### L2-AER-FC-060

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-060
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     When a run starts, the flight control subsystem shall take the number, the directions
  of action, the points of application and the output limits of the effectors from the vehicle
  parameter set of the run.
Rationale:     The effector layout (number, directions, points of application) and the limits are
  EF parameters that a campaign can change without a software change (L2-AER-EF-050, DD-200).
  FC allocates against that layout, so it reads it from the parameter set at the start and
  holds no copy in its design (IN-C3-20). Then a different layout, or a different control law
  with the same parameter set, changes no vehicle-model file and no requirement. The output limits
  are the ones the database defines for each effector, which L1-AER-110 names. The layout is
  constant over a run (L2-AER-EF-020).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-400): the replacement of the design leaves the vehicle model unchanged and,
  by this record, a change of the vehicle parameter set leaves FC unchanged.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-110): with L2-AER-EF-010 (the maximum force of each effector is a
  parameter), FC uses the limit the database defines for each effector, not a value of its own.
```

### L2-AER-FC-070

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-070
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     The flight control subsystem shall issue the effector commands of each control frame,
  computed from the vehicle state of the first simulation step of that frame, for use from that
  step.
Rationale:     Flight control computes the effector commands at the start of each control frame from
  the state of its first step (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2) and EF uses them from the first step
  of that frame (L2-AER-EF-150). Flight control adds no delay of its own in simulation time, so
  the first change of effector command that responds to a vehicle state comes at the next frame
  start, at most one control frame later (DD-020). The first control frame of a run starts at
  step zero (IN-C7-32, DD-C7-02).

  change of effector command is at most [P-SYS-310]; EF brings the output to the command
  within [P-EF-010] (L2-AER-EF-340); and [P-SYS-310] plus [P-EF-010] is at most [P-SYS-480], so
  the first change of output responds within the latency budget.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-680): the control frame period is the flight software period, an integer
  multiple of the simulation step; FC runs once in each frame, as this record and L2-AER-FC-080
  state.
```

### L2-AER-FC-100

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-100
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While the vehicle is airborne with fuel remaining and the axis references do not carry
  the off value, the flight control subsystem shall keep each translational axis within the
  tracking tolerance of that axis and regulated quantity of its axis reference.
Rationale:     The accuracy of the translational regulation: the north, east and vertical axes follow
  the position or the velocity that autoflight asks for. The tracking tolerance depends on the
  axis group (horizontal or vertical) and on the regulated quantity (position or velocity); TR
  states the four values as parameters, as L1-AER-370 asks of the FC children, together with the
  speed ranges, the wind and the settling time. The statement is limited to airborne flight with
  fuel, because on the ground the ground reaction holds the vehicle and after fuel exhaustion the
  effectors give no force (D68).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-310): L3-AER-FC-TR-020 keeps the horizontal velocity within [P-FC-040] of the
  reference for horizontal speeds up to [P-SYS-230]; the authority for it is EF's (L2-AER-EF-330).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-320, L1-AER-330): L3-AER-FC-TR-030 keeps the vertical velocity within
  [P-FC-050] of the reference for climb rates up to [P-SYS-240] and descent rates up to [P-SYS-250].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-460): L3-AER-FC-TR-040 keeps the horizontal position within [P-FC-010] of the
  reference position; with the AF share [P-AF-160] the sum is at most [P-SYS-450] (closure rule,
  FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.4); L3-AER-FC-TR-050 does the same for the altitude.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-970): at the touchdown the vertical speed is within [P-FC-050] of the
  descent rate [P-AF-240] and the horizontal speed within [P-FC-040] of the reference, so with
  the AF hover tolerance [P-AF-170] the touchdown speeds are inside the landing envelope
  ([P-SYS-500], [P-SYS-510]) when [P-AF-240] plus [P-FC-050] is at most [P-SYS-500] and the sum
  of the horizontal shares is at most [P-SYS-510] (closure at I1).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-370): L3-AER-FC-TR-060 gives the settling time of a reference step for each
  of the four cases, so each translational axis settles on its reference.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-310, L1-AER-320, L1-AER-330, L1-AER-370, L1-AER-460, L1-AER-970
```

### L2-AER-FC-110

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-110
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While the vehicle is airborne with fuel remaining and the axis references do not carry
  the off value, the flight control subsystem shall keep each rotational axis within the tracking
  tolerance of that axis of its axis reference.
Rationale:     The accuracy of the rotational regulation: the heading follows the reference heading
  and its rate (a change of heading in place, or at speed), and pitch and roll stay at their
  references, which are zero (D57). RO states the tolerances as parameters (L1-AER-370). The
  vehicle has no aerodynamic moment (DD-050), so there is no disturbance moment to reject; the
  moments only follow the references. The statement is limited to airborne flight with fuel for
  the reason given in L2-AER-FC-100.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-350): L3-AER-FC-RO-030 keeps the heading rate within [P-FC-080] of the
  reference for heading rates up to [P-SYS-270], at maximum inertia; the moment authority is
  EF's (L2-AER-EF-320), and L3-AER-FC-RO-060 scales the moment demand with the inertia of the
  frame.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-390): the attitude references come from AF and are zero for pitch and roll;
  L3-AER-FC-RO-040 holds pitch and roll within [P-AF-180] of them, which is FC's share of
  L1-AER-390, AF's being to set the references.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-370): L3-AER-FC-RO-020 keeps the heading within [P-FC-070] of the reference;
  L3-AER-FC-RO-050 gives the settling time of a reference step; L3-AER-FC-RO-070 makes the turn
  the shorter way round (CV-930).
Parent(s):     L1-AER-350, L1-AER-370, L1-AER-390
Source:        N-170, N-240; D57; CV-930
Allocated to:  RO
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-110; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    P-FC-070, P-FC-080, P-SYS-270, P-AF-180
TBD/TBR:       P-FC-070, P-FC-080 values TBR (TT-C4-1), P-AF-180 value TBR (TT-190), P-SYS-270 value
  TBR (TT-010), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
```

### L2-AER-FC-120

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-120
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While no axis demand is limited, when the axis reference of one axis changes, the
  flight control subsystem shall keep the velocity along each other translational axis within
  [P-SYS-540] of its reference rate and the body rate about each other rotational axis within
  [P-SYS-550] of its reference rate.
Rationale:     The measurable form of independent control (L0-CON-020) and of the absence of
  cross-axis coupling (L0-CON-030, D18): a manoeuvre on one axis leaves the other five where they
  were. The translational axes are those of the references, the axes of the vehicle-carried
  local frame V (DD-C4-01); with the heading at zero they are the body axes, and at another
  heading the body components of a steady reference turn with the heading, which the force
  resolution of TR follows (DD-C4-02). L1-AER-380 says "body axis" and CR-C4-03 aligns the
  wording. The tolerances are the cross-axis budget that FC shares with EF; EF adds no cross
  response of its own (L2-AER-EF-040), so they depend on FC alone. The statement is limited to
  steps that no demand limit touches: a limited axis demand is the nominal saturation of
  L2-AER-FC-170.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-380): the six axes of L2-AER-FC-010 are regulated separately
  (L3-AER-FC-TR-010, L3-AER-FC-RO-010) and this record bounds the departure of the other five from
  their references for a step on each in turn, which is the L1 test.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-380
Source:        N-240; D18; L1-AER-380 rationale
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-120; success criteria: in still air, for a step of the axis reference of
  each of the six axes in turn (translational steps from hover and from a horizontal speed of a
  tenth of [P-SYS-230], at headings of zero and of a quarter turn; rotational steps of the
  heading and of the pitch and roll angles), sized so that no axis demand is limited, the
  velocity along each other translational axis stays within [P-SYS-540] of its reference rate and
  the body rate about each other rotational axis stays within [P-SYS-550] of its reference rate
  at each control frame, at full fuel and near dry mass.
Parameters:    P-SYS-540, P-SYS-550, P-SYS-230
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-540, P-SYS-550 values TBR (TT-100, TT-C4-1), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-130

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-130
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While the vehicle is airborne with fuel remaining, the flight control subsystem shall
  limit the magnitude of the vehicle acceleration that it demands from the translational axes to
  [P-SYS-440].
Rationale:     The crew limit (L0-CON-310, D38, D45) is a limit on the acceleration the crew senses,
  which is the vehicle acceleration minus the gravity vector, so its magnitude is at most the
  vehicle acceleration plus [P-DYN-010] (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2). Applying the limit to the
  vehicle acceleration that FC demands, and converting it to a force with the current vehicle mass
  (L3-AER-FC-TR-090), makes the allowed resultant force fall with the mass as D38 asks and leaves
  the drag, which the control force must also overcome, outside the limit (DD-C4-04). A limit on
  the resultant force alone, at the mass times [P-SYS-120], would make the maximum speed
  unreachable against the drag of L1-AER-130. FC meets the demand limit even when a large
  position or velocity error asks for more, as nominal saturation (N-260). The demand is not the
  vehicle acceleration: the part of the vehicle acceleration that a disturbance adds is bounded
  by L2-AER-FC-140.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-290): the closure rule [P-DYN-010] plus [P-SYS-440] at most [P-SYS-120]
  (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2) makes the sensed acceleration at most [P-SYS-120] when the vehicle
  acceleration is at most [P-SYS-440]; this record limits the demand to it and L2-AER-FC-140
  bounds the disturbance residual, with AF's reference acceleration at most [P-AF-200].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-300): this record is FC's hard limit and L2-AER-FC-140 its transient
  allowance; AF's share is [P-AF-200], and [P-AF-200] plus [P-FC-020] is at most [P-SYS-440].
Parent(s):     L1-AER-290, L1-AER-300
Source:        D38, D45; N-170; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.1 (upper side), §7.2; DD-C4-04
Allocated to:  TR
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-130; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    P-SYS-440, P-SYS-120, P-DYN-010, P-AF-200, P-FC-020
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-440, P-AF-200, P-FC-020 values TBR (TT-040), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-140

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-140
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While the vehicle is airborne with fuel remaining in a wind within the wind envelope,
  when the magnitude of the acceleration of the translational reference rates is at most
  [P-AF-200], the flight control subsystem shall keep the magnitude of the difference between the
  vehicle acceleration and that reference acceleration at most [P-FC-020].
Rationale:     The split of the vehicle-acceleration budget (L1-AER-300, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2):
  autoflight commands at most [P-AF-200] and the transients of flight control add at most
  [P-FC-020], their sum being at most [P-SYS-440]. The reference acceleration is the rate of
  change of the translational reference rates, resolved in the local frame V (the reference
  acceleration of the axis references). The difference covers the correction FC adds for an
  error and the acceleration a disturbance causes before FC compensates: a gust that builds up
  over at least [P-SYS-570] changes the drag at a bounded rate (D62), the liftoff of a takeoff in
  the wind [P-SYS-580] gives a step of drag (D73), and both are compensated within the control-loop
  latency [P-SYS-480] and a short settling. TR states both cases (L3-AER-FC-TR-080, -100).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-300): AF's reference acceleration is at most [P-AF-200] by its own record;
  with this record the vehicle acceleration is at most [P-AF-200] plus [P-FC-020], which is at
  most [P-SYS-440]; L2-AER-FC-130 limits the demand beyond the nominal envelope.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-300
Source:        D16, D51, D62, D73; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2; IN-C1-34
Allocated to:  TR
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-140; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    P-AF-200, P-FC-020, P-SYS-440, P-SYS-570, P-SYS-580, P-SYS-480
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-440, P-AF-200, P-FC-020 values TBR (TT-040), P-SYS-570 value TBR (TT-650),
  P-SYS-580 value TBR (TT-860), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-150

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-150
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the effector activation state is on, the flight control subsystem shall allocate
  the axis demands to effector commands whose resultant control force and control moment equal the
  axis demands, for each set of axis demands within the authority box.
Rationale:     F-AER-06: the six axis demands (a force along and a moment about each body axis) become
  one command for each effector, such that the effectors' resultant is the demand. The authority
  box is the set of combinations that EF guarantees together (L2-AER-EF-330, DD-170), and it
  contains the six pure authorities of L1-AER-120 to -140 (L2-AER-EF-300 to -320), so a demand
  with one component at the limit and the other five at zero is a set within the box. The
  resultant is computed from the commands through the control effectiveness of the vehicle
  parameter set (L2-AER-EF-010, -120); the effector lag is EF's (L2-AER-EF-340). AL states the
  allocation accuracy as parameters.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-120): L2-AER-EF-300 makes the pure upward force of [P-SYS-400] exist within
  the output limits and this record, through L3-AER-FC-AL-010, commands it, so the Aeronef can
  produce it; FC's share is the allocation, EF's the existence.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-130): the same for the five other directions and [P-SYS-410]
  (L2-AER-EF-310).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-140): the same for each pure moment and [P-SYS-420] (L2-AER-EF-320).
Parent(s):     L1-AER-120, L1-AER-130, L1-AER-140
Source:        FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS F-AER-06, §7.1; D46, D72; L2-AER-EF-300 to -330
Allocated to:  AL
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-150; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    P-SYS-400, P-SYS-410, P-SYS-420
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-400, P-SYS-410, P-SYS-420 values TBR (TT-020), Martin, G2 (I1)
```

### L2-AER-FC-160

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-160
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The flight control subsystem shall command each effector within the output limits of
  that effector.
Rationale:     Each effector has a maximum force (L0-CON-300, L1-AER-110). EF clips a command beyond a
  limit (L2-AER-EF-110), so a command beyond it would be harmless to the vehicle but would hide a
  demand that cannot be produced; FC does not rely on output beyond the limits (IN-C3-21).
  The limits are the smallest output [P-EF-130] and the largest output [P-EF-120] of each effector,
  read from the vehicle parameter set (L2-AER-FC-060). The statement holds with the effectors on
  and off, and in the stress case of a demand beyond the authority box (L2-AER-FC-170).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-110): EF enforces the limit at the effector and this record keeps FC's
  commands within it; together every effector stays within its maximum, as L0-CON-300 asks.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-110
Source:        L0-CON-300; IN-C3-21; owner review at G0
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-160; success criteria: in each automated test run of the regression
  campaign and in a run whose scripted references demand more than the authority box, each
  recorded effector command lies within the output limits of its effector at each control frame.
Parameters:    P-EF-120, P-EF-130
TBD/TBR:       P-EF-120, P-EF-130 values TBR (TT-930), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-170

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-170
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When an axis demand lies outside the authority box, the flight control subsystem shall
  allocate that axis demand limited to the authority box, with the effector activation state
  unchanged.
Rationale:     Saturation is nominal behaviour, not a failure (N-260, L1-AER-110 rationale): the
  Aeronef does something defined when a demand exceeds what the effectors can produce. The
  defined behaviour is to limit the demand to the box that EF guarantees, so the limited demand is
  always producible (L2-AER-EF-330), and to carry on. No annunciation, no failure state and no
  change of the activation state follow. The rule that limits the components is a derived
  requirement: each demand component on its own, the others unchanged (L3-AER-FC-AL-020, DD-C4-03).
  The limits of the crew g-limit (L2-AER-FC-130) act earlier, on the acceleration demand.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-110): L2-AER-FC-160 keeps every command within the output limits and this
  record gives the demand beyond them a defined, producible meaning, so a demand beyond the limit
  is handled as nominal behaviour, as the L1 rationale asks; the priority rule is the derived
  requirement.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-110
Source:        N-260; L1-AER-110 rationale; CONOPS §8.2; D4
Allocated to:  AL
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-170; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-180

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-180
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a limited axis demand returns within its limit, the flight control subsystem
  shall bring that axis within its tracking tolerance, crossing its reference by at most that
  tolerance, within the settling time of that axis.
Rationale:     Saturation as nominal behaviour (N-260) includes the way out of it: a regulator that
  keeps accumulating an error while its demand is limited overshoots long after the limit has
  gone (integrator windup). The requirement is the observable form of a regulation that is aware
  of its limit: once the demand is back within its range the axis settles within the settling
  time of its group (TR, RO) and does not cross the reference by more than its tolerance. How the
  regulation achieves it (back-calculation, conditional integration) is a design decision
  (DD-C4-07). "Limited" covers the demand limit of the effector set (L2-AER-FC-170) and the crew
  limit (L2-AER-FC-130).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-110): the demand beyond the limit is limited (L2-AER-FC-170) and the axis
  recovers (this record), so the nominal behaviour is defined from the onset of the saturation to
  its end.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-370): the settling of an axis after a step is L3-AER-FC-TR-060 and
  L3-AER-FC-RO-050; this record extends it to the step that saturates, so each axis settles on its
  reference in all the cases of the L1 test.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-110, L1-AER-370
Source:        N-260; CONOPS §8.2
Allocated to:  TR, RO
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FC-180; success criteria: each child requirement has passed its
  verification and the decomposition has been reviewed (D40).
Parameters:    P-FC-060, P-FC-090
TBD/TBR:       P-FC-060, P-FC-090 values TBR (TT-C4-1), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-190

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-190
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the axis references carry the off value, the flight control subsystem shall set
  the effector activation state to off and each effector command to zero.
Rationale:     D69: on the ground the effectors are off, so that no fuel is used (L1-AER-930,
  L1-AER-940). Autoflight sends the off value on the ground, after a Land touchdown and at a ground
  start, and the off value carries no mode information (L1-AER-420). FC derives the activation
  state from the off value alone (DD-C4-06): off while it is received, on while it is not
  (L2-AER-FC-200). Both the state and a zero command are set because the effector lag decays to
  zero without reaching it (L2-AER-EF-210). Timing of a landing: a contact at some step of a
  frame is seen in the gear state of the next frame start, at most [P-SYS-310] later; autoflight
  sets the off value in that frame, before FC runs (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2); FC sets the state
  off in the same frame; EF gives zero output from its first step (L2-AER-EF-210). The effector
  outputs are therefore zero within [P-SYS-310] of the contact, inside the [P-SYS-480] of
  L1-AER-930. Until then the commanded upward force stays below the weight (L2-AER-FC-210).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-930): AF sets the off value (its child), this record switches the state off
  and commands zero, and L2-AER-EF-210 makes each output zero, so the effectors are off in the
  on-ground state of L1-AER-930; the takeoff is the other case (L2-AER-FC-200).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-950): a run on the ground starts with the off value from the first control
  frame (AF's child), so FC commands zero and EF produces nothing from step zero.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-930, L1-AER-950
Source:        D69, D73; CONOPS §4.5, SC-130; IN-C3-26; DD-C4-06
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-190; success criteria: in a ground start, and in each landing of the
  takeoff and landing tests, at each control frame that carries the off value the recorded
  effector activation state is off and every recorded effector command is zero; and each effector
  output is zero at each step from [P-SYS-480] after the contact step of a landing, or from step
  zero of a ground start, until the references leave the off value.
Parameters:    P-SYS-310, P-SYS-480
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-310, P-SYS-480 values TBR (TT-070), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-200

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-200
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the axis references change from the off value to axis references, the flight
  control subsystem shall set the effector activation state to on in the control frame that
  carries them.
Rationale:     A takeoff powers up the effectors (D69, glossary *Takeoff*): autoflight engages the
  takeoff and replaces the off value by axis references that hold the position and call for a
  climb, and FC switches the effectors on in the same frame (DD-C4-06). It is the only time the
  effectors work on the ground (L1-AER-930), and the state stays on until the off value returns.
  An airborne test start (D73, B3-1) has axis references from the first frame, so the state is on
  from step zero. Fuel exhaustion needs no case here: EF gives zero output whatever the state
  (L2-AER-EF-200, D68).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-960): the climb of the takeoff needs the effectors on; this record is FC's
  share of the power-up, AF's being the engagement and the references, and L2-AER-FC-100 and -220
  the tracking and the position in the liftoff wind.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-930): the effectors work on the ground only while the references are not
  the off value, which is this record with L2-AER-FC-190.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-960, L1-AER-930
Source:        D69; IN-C3-26, IN-C3-30; DD-C4-06
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-200; success criteria: in a ground start in which the first axis
  references that are not the off value are sent at a frame start, the recorded effector
  activation state is on in that control frame and the earlier ones are off, and the vehicle
  lifts off.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-210

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-210
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the gear state shows the landing gear extended and the reference rate of the
  vertical axis is below zero, the flight control subsystem shall keep the upward control force
  that it commands at most the product of the vehicle mass and [P-DYN-010] reduced by the fraction
  [P-FC-120].
Rationale:     The liftoff rule of the ground contact model: the vehicle leaves the ground at the
  first step at which the upward control force exceeds the weight (MS-DYN §9.6, IN-C1-43). A Land
  descent ends at a contact in some step of a control frame, and the commands of that frame were
  computed before the contact, when the gear state could not yet show it. If those commands carry
  more than the weight, the vehicle lifts off again and makes a second landing. FC therefore
  caps the upward force as long as the gear is extended and the vertical reference rate is
  negative: that is the descent to touchdown, and nothing else in flight, because the gear is
  retracted in flight except between a liftoff and the retraction, when the reference rate is
  positive or zero, and in the Hover that precedes the Land, when it is zero (DD-C4-05). The
  fraction [P-FC-120] keeps the cap below the weight at the rounding of the model; the steady
  descent needs a force below the weight by the drag only, so the cap does not bind in a nominal
  descent. FC reads the gear state only here (CR-C4-02); the contact itself is detected by DYN and
  autoflight, and FC does not detect it.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-970): the L1 test requires one landing event per landing and no crash;
  with the touchdown speeds of L2-AER-FC-100 inside the landing envelope, this record prevents
  the liftoff after the contact, so the landing is a single event.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-930): the effectors are off after a landing (L2-AER-FC-190); this record
  keeps the vehicle on the ground in the frame before they are, so that the ground state of
  L1-AER-930 holds.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-970, L1-AER-930
Source:        IN-C1-43; L1-AER-930 rationale; MS-DYN §9.6; DD-C4-05
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-210; success criteria: in each landing from Hover (full fuel and near
  dry mass, in still air and in a steady wind of [P-SYS-430] with gusts of [P-SYS-560] built up
  over [P-SYS-570], from several directions) exactly one landing event occurs and no liftoff
  event follows it; and in a scripted run on the extended gear with a descent reference and a
  vertical speed error that would call for more than the weight, the recorded upward component
  of the commanded control force never exceeds the cap.
Parameters:    P-DYN-010, P-FC-120, P-SYS-430, P-SYS-560, P-SYS-570
TBD/TBR:       P-FC-120 value TBR (TT-C4-1), P-SYS-430, P-SYS-560 values TBR (TT-030), P-SYS-570
  value TBR (TT-650), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-220

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-220
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     When the vehicle lifts off in a wind of speed at most [P-SYS-580], the flight control
  subsystem shall keep the horizontal distance between the vehicle position and the reference
  position within [P-SYS-450] at each control frame of the interval of [P-FC-060] that starts at
  the liftoff.
Rationale:     On the ground the ground reaction holds the vehicle against the drag of the wind and
  the flight software does not know the wind (L1-AER-670, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2). At liftoff
  that drag acts at once, as a step, and the takeoff has to keep the position within the
  budget of L1-AER-960 while the vehicle climbs to the hover height. The reference position of a
  takeoff is the position on the ground (AF), so the guidance share of the budget is zero and FC's
  share is [P-SYS-450]; the interval [P-FC-060] is the translational settling time, long enough
  for the climb to the hover height at [P-AF-240] (a fraction of a second) and for the gear
  retraction. The acceleration the step causes is within [P-FC-020] at dry mass for the limit
  [P-SYS-580] (L3-AER-FC-TR-100); after the interval L3-AER-FC-TR-040 applies.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-960): the effectors come on (L2-AER-FC-200), the vehicle climbs at the
  reference rate (L3-AER-FC-TR-030) and this record keeps the position in the wind of the liftoff;
  AF's share is the references, the gear retraction and the handover to Hover.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-960
Source:        D69, D73 (B3-Q2); FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2; CONOPS SC-130
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FC-220; success criteria: from ground starts at full fuel and near dry mass,
  in still air and in a steady wind of [P-SYS-580] from several directions, with a takeoff engaged,
  the horizontal distance between the recorded position and the position on the ground is at most
  [P-SYS-450] at each output sample from the liftoff until [P-FC-060] after it.
Parameters:    P-SYS-580, P-SYS-450, P-FC-060, P-AF-240, P-FC-020
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-580 value TBR (TT-860), P-SYS-450 value TBR (TT-050), P-AF-240 value TBR
  (TT-830), P-FC-060 value TBR (TT-C4-1), P-FC-020 value TBR (TT-040), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-240

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-240
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     When the horizontal position and the altitude are held in still air at level attitude,
  the flight control subsystem shall command a resultant control force and control moment whose
  fuel mass, by the consumption relationship of MS-DYN, over a hold of duration [P-DYN-270] is at
  most the product of the sum of one and [P-FU-100] with the fuel mass of a control force equal to
  the vehicle weight over the same time.
Rationale:     Fuel budget (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.3, L1-AER-360, CR-C4-01). The endurance is sized
  for a hold in which the control force only balances the weight; the margin [P-FU-100] covers the
  regulation of the hold, the moments that keep the attitude, the transient at the start and the
  takeoff (L2-AER-FU-290). EF adds nothing to it (the lag has unit gain and no overshoot, the
  resultant is the sum of the outputs: IN-C3-24). FC's commanded resultant is therefore the quantity
  that spends the margin, and this record states that it stays inside it. The consumption relationship
  is on the magnitude of the resultant force and of the resultant moment (DD-110), so the allocation
  of the resultant to the effectors changes nothing. The share of the margin the takeoff uses is
  a few hundredths of a second of hover (L2-AER-FU-290).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-360): FU sizes the capacity for the margin (L2-AER-FU-290) and EF produces the
  commanded resultant exactly; this record keeps the commanded resultant inside the margin, so
  the endurance holds. It is FC's child once CR-C4-01 allocates L1-AER-360 to FC.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-360
Source:        IN-C2-23, IN-C3-24; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.3; L2-AER-FU-290; D74
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-FC-240; success criteria: in a simulated run from a ground start at full
  fuel in still air, with a takeoff engaged at the start and Hover then kept over [P-DYN-270]
  (the run of AN-FU-290), the fuel mass that the consumption relationship of MS-DYN gives for the
  recorded effector commands applied to the effectors' control effectiveness is at most the stated
  product, from the control frame at which the vertical axis first reaches its tolerance of the hover
  height to the end of the hold, and the fuel mass of the takeoff is at most the margin share that
  L2-AER-FU-290 gives it.
Parameters:    P-FU-100, P-DYN-270, P-FU-040, P-FU-050, P-DYN-010
TBD/TBR:       P-FU-100 value TBR (TT-790), P-DYN-270 value TBR (TT-760), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FC-260

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FC-260
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The flight control subsystem shall compute each effector command only from the axis
  references, the vehicle state, the gear state, the mass properties, the vehicle parameter set and
  the configuration of the run, and from its own state at the preceding control frames of the run.
Rationale:     Repeat runs must give the same pass or fail verdicts (L1-AER-690, D59, D63 Q4). FC adds
  no variation if its commands depend on nothing but the run's inputs and its own memory of the run:
  no wall-clock time, no random source and no state carried from another run or another simulation
  instance. Bit-identical results are not required (D25, D59).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): FC's commands are part of the operational data and the inputs of EF;
  this record makes them a function of the run's inputs, so a repeated run gives the same FC
  signals and verdicts, which is FC's share.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-690
Source:        N-330; D25, D59, D63

  condition, selection or reference script and TST step and wind inputs, in two simulation
```

<!-- END SECTION FC -->

<!-- BEGIN SECTION AF -->
## AF — Autoflight

The autoflight requirements (task C5). The subject of each statement is the autoflight subsystem (AF), one of the four flight-software subsystems with FCP, NAV and FC (D55). AF implements F-AER-07 and F-AER-08 and part of F-AER-14 (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §6.1): it decides which modes are armed and active, turns the crew's selections into references for the six degrees of freedom (guidance), commands the landing gear and gives the annunciation content. It runs once per control frame, after NAV and before FC (DD-010). Inputs: the mode selections, the selector targets and the accepted selectors from FCP; the navigation quantities from NAV; the gear state from DYN and the fuel state from FU; the initial mode state. Outputs: the axis references to FC, the landing gear command to DYN, the annunciation content to FCP, and the mode state, targets and gear command to TM.

**The mode logic is a specification of its own.** The state, the decisions on the eight mode selections in each state, the arming and the captures, the handovers, the target rules, the heading behaviour in each mode, the gear rules, the fuel exhaustion and the valid initial states are in `specs/AF_MODE_LOGIC.md` (draft `work/C5/AF_MODE_LOGIC.md`), as tables with row IDs (DT, TR, TG, PR, HD, GR, IC). METHODOLOGY §8.3 prescribes this for mode logic: a short requirement that references a decision table. These records point to it by name, because a statement cannot carry a section number, and L2-AER-AF-010 makes it binding on every autoflight implementation (D15). The catalogue is D56 extended by D69: Stationary, Heading, Altitude hold, Go to altitude, Vertical speed, Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff and Land; one horizontal and one vertical mode are active at a time, and the last four govern both axes. Pitch and roll stay at zero (D57).

**What AF guarantees and what FC guarantees.** AF shapes the references (position and velocity, altitude and vertical speed, heading and heading rate, level pitch and roll) within the acceleration limits, and FC tracks them (L2-AER-AF-090; the L1 budgets split this way: [P-AF-160] and [P-FC-010]). The records of AF's own outputs are therefore about the references and the mode state. The tolerances of the vehicle in each mode ([P-AF-090] to [P-AF-130], [P-AF-170]) are vehicle-level statements with `Allocated to: FC, EF`; the split of each between the AF reference and the FC tracking is for Phase 4.

**Allocation and Phase 4.** A mode record with `Allocated to: AF` has its children in the per-mode L3 sections of Phase 4 (M0 Stationary as the pattern, then M2, M3, M5, M8), which refine the engagement, capture shape, stopping profile, tolerances and flow-down inside the rows of AF_MODE_LOGIC. Until those sections exist the trace check shows these records as childless, as it does for L1 parents before L2. A record with `Allocated to: —` is a leaf at L2 and is verified directly by its own method.

**Verification.** Procedure IDs follow the record number (TP-, AN-, IN-, DM-AF-nnn); Phase 5 (V1) writes them. Most are tests on the TST with a scripted selection sequence and the operational data (mode state, targets, axis references, gear command), the four things L0-OBJ-140 relates. Two tests read the interface itself (ICD-FCP, ICD-FC-AF) and need the TST to record the flight-software interfaces (IN-C7-40, IN-C8-40). The decisions and transitions of AF_MODE_LOGIC are covered by TP-AF-010, -120 and -300 row by row.

**Ground operations, fuel exhaustion and the panel.** On the ground no mode is active and only Takeoff is accepted (L2-AER-AF-140, -750); Land ends in that state (L2-AER-AF-270). At fuel exhaustion autoflight disengages and refuses every selection (L2-AER-AF-860, -870, D68). The panel has no Hover selection and no disengage selection (D71): Hover is left by a selection of a group mode (L2-AER-AF-190), and there is no crew disengage, since with no manual control a disengaged airborne vehicle would have nothing flying it.

**Coverage of the L1 parents.** Every live L1 record allocated to AF has at least one AF child. L1-AER-340 is Deleted (D56) and has none. L1-AER-360 (endurance) is allocated to FU and EF only; the AF share of its premise is the constant references of Hover (L2-AER-AF-640).

| L1 parent | What AF guarantees, in the parent's terms | AF children |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L1-AER-290 | The reference acceleration is bounded by the manoeuvre limit, the AF share of the crew budget; FC bounds its transients, FU supplies the mass, DYN the sensed acceleration | -420 |
| L1-AER-300 | The manoeuvre acceleration of the translational reference (horizontal, turning and vertical) is at most the AF share, with the heading-rate bound closing the turn case (D72 Q1) | -420, -430, -440 |
| L1-AER-310 | Heading commands each horizontal speed from zero up to the maximum and holds it within the speed tolerance; FC and EF sustain it | -530, -610 |

| L1-AER-330 | The same for each descent speed up to the maximum; the level-off at the selected altitude is the capture | -320, -430, -600, -620 |
| L1-AER-350 | Heading commands each heading rate up to the maximum where the speed allows, by the shorter way, and the bound keeps the lateral acceleration in budget | -440, -520 |
| L1-AER-390 | AF gives zero pitch and roll references in every mode while engaged; FC holds the angles within the tolerance | -400 |
| L1-AER-410 | The implementation is the one the configuration names | -020 |

| L1-AER-430 | AF gives the gear command and takes the gear state and the fuel state, and nothing else crosses | -060 |
| L1-AER-450 | Every mode of the catalogue (nine) has a mode record; the mode state, the exclusivity, the decisions on selections, the arming, the capture, the handovers, the targets and the heading behaviour are stated; the logic is in AF_MODE_LOGIC | -010, -100, -110, -120, -130, -170, -180, -190, -220, -230, -240, -260, -300, -310, -350, -360, -450, -500, -520, -530, -540, -560, -580, -590, -620, -630, -640 |

| L1-AER-680 | AF executes once per frame in the order of the frame | -070 |

Two records are Derived: L2-AER-AF-860 and -870 (DD-C5-05, the fuel exhaustion rule of D68). The design decisions proposed for AF are DD-C5-01 to DD-C5-07 (`work/C5/issues.md` §5). The records in this draft: 73 (Constraint 5, Functional 46, Interface 10, Performance 12); verification: Analysis 3, Demonstration 1, Inspection 5, Test 64.

### L2-AER-AF-010

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-010
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall realise the mode state, the decisions on mode
  selections, the automatic transitions, the target rules and the annunciation content that
  AF_MODE_LOGIC specifies.
Rationale:     Modes and controllers are decoupled (D15, D33): every autoflight implementation meets
  the same mode logic, so any implementation pairs with any control law. AF_MODE_LOGIC is that one
  place, and the per-mode sections of Phase 4 refine inside it. L1-AER-450 asks that the mode logic
  be specified in AF_MODE_LOGIC and that each mode of the catalogue be covered by an AF
  requirement; this record gives the specification its binding force, and the mode records below
  cover the catalogue.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with the selection, transition and mode records, this record covers
  the nine modes of D56 and D69, their arming, their transitions and their annunciation.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D15, D33, D56, D69; AF_MODE_LOGIC
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-010; success criteria: a scripted sequence that covers each row of the
  tables DT, TR, TG, PR, HD, GR and IC of AF_MODE_LOGIC gives, at the control frame that follows the
  selection, the mode state, the targets and the landing gear command that the row states, in the
  operational data.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall compute each output of a control frame only from the
  run's configuration, the initial condition, the inputs of that frame and the state it held at the
  end of the previous frame.
Rationale:     Reproducible runs (L0-OBJ-110, L1-AER-690, D59): repeated runs of the same
  configuration, initial condition and selection script give the same pass or fail verdicts. AF adds
  no wall-clock time, no random number and no input other than those listed, so the only variation
  is that of the other subsystems.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): each subsystem has a reproducibility child; this is the AF one.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-690
Source:        D25, D59
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-080; success criteria: two executions of the same scripted run give the
  same sequence of mode states, the same decisions on mode selections and axis references that agree
  within the tolerance of each success criterion assessed on the run.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-090

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-090
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall give flight control, for each of the six degrees of
  freedom, one axis reference made of a reference value, the rate of that value and an indication of
  whether flight control regulates the value.
Rationale:     The glossary leaves "which quantity each reference is" to C4 and C5. Autoflight shapes
  the reference (position and velocity for the horizontal and vertical axes, heading and heading
  rate, and level pitch and roll) within the acceleration limits, and flight control tracks it; this
  is why the position-holding budget splits into an AF share [P-AF-160] and an FC share [P-FC-010]
  (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.4). The indication separates a hold (the value is regulated) from a motion
  at a rate (only the rate is regulated, as in a climb at a selected vertical speed). The frame and
  the exact items are for ICD-FC-AF (DD-C5-01, C5-Q12).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-420): with L2-AER-AF-030 this defines what crosses the one interface.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-420
Source:        D15, D56; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.4; DD-C5-01
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-AF-090; success criteria: ICD-FC-AF defines, for each of the six
  degrees of freedom, a reference value, its rate and the regulation indication, and the AF
  implementation gives each of the three at each control frame.
Parameters:    P-AF-160, P-FC-010
TBD/TBR:       ICD-FC-AF content (C5-Q12), Martin, G2 (I1 with C4)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-100

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-100
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall keep, at each control frame, a mode state made of the
  engaged indication, the active horizontal mode, the active vertical mode, the active both-axes mode
  and the armed mode, with the values and invariants that AF_MODE_LOGIC lists.
Rationale:     The mode state is one data item with a fixed set of values, the same for every
  implementation (IN-C7-31, D15). Its fields follow the mode groups of D56 and the both-axes modes
  of D69; the armed mode is one field because Altitude hold and Hover cannot be armed together
  (invariant MS-4). The state is what FCP annunciates (L1-AER-470) and what TM records
  (L1-AER-620).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the item states the "which modes are active and armed" of the
  catalogue; the rows of AF_MODE_LOGIC and the records below say how it changes.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D69; AF_MODE_LOGIC §3
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-100; success criteria: in every test run, at every output sample, the
  recorded mode state has only the values of AF_MODE_LOGIC §3 and meets the invariants MS-1 to MS-5.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-110

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-110
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While autoflight is engaged, the autoflight subsystem shall have either one active
  mode in each of the horizontal group and the vertical group, or one active both-axes mode and no
  active mode in either group.
Rationale:     Owner decision D56 (Q5, #6): one horizontal and one vertical mode are active at a time,
  independently, and Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff and Land are the only modes that govern both axes
  (extended by D69). Each group always has a mode while engaged (A2-Q05): a hold mode covers a
  group the crew has not chosen (rules DT-12 to DT-16).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the exclusivity rule is the "mode combination" content of the
  catalogue.

  engaged, either both the active horizontal mode and the active vertical mode are not none and the
  active both-axes mode is none, or the active both-axes mode is not none and both groups are none.
```

### L2-AER-AF-120

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-120
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     At each control frame, the autoflight subsystem shall decide each mode selection that
  the flight control panel gives it, in the order given, as accepted or refused according to the
  decisions of AF_MODE_LOGIC.
Rationale:     FCP filters nothing (IN-C8-01): AF decides for each of the eight mode selections in each
  mode state, so the mode logic is in one place. The order is the order of the timestamps, and of
  receipt for equal timestamps (L2-AER-FCP-090); each decision sees the state that the previous one
  left, so a script that selects two modes in one frame has one defined result.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the decision table covers every pair of mode state class and mode
  selection (25 rows for 8 selections).
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D15, D16; IN-C8-01, -04
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-120; success criteria: a scripted selection for each row DT-01 to DT-25
  of AF_MODE_LOGIC, from the state class of the row, is accepted or refused as the row states; two
  selections of one frame give the state of the rows applied in order.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-130

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-130
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     If the autoflight subsystem refuses a mode selection, then the autoflight subsystem
  shall leave the mode state, the axis references and the landing gear command as they would be
  without that selection.
Rationale:     A refused selection must be harmless: the panel offers every mode selection in every
  state, and a selection that does not fit the state (Land in flight, Takeoff in the air) is not an
  error (CONOPS SC-130). The crew is told nothing extra: a refusal shows as an unchanged mode state.
  The decision is recorded for the tests (L2-AER-AF-840).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with the decision table, a refusal has one defined result.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D71; IN-C8-01
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-130; success criteria: after each refused selection of TP-AF-120 the
  mode state, the axis references and the landing gear command equal those of a run without that
  selection.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-140

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-140
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the vehicle is on the ground and Takeoff is not active, the autoflight subsystem
  shall refuse each mode selection other than Takeoff.
Rationale:     On the ground the effectors are off and no mode is active (D69, L1-AER-930, -950): the
  seven other modes need a vehicle that is flying. The alternative, arming them for after the
  takeoff, would let a stale selection act at a later time (C5-Q03). Land is refused because it is
  engaged only from Hover.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-950): with L2-AER-AF-750 the ground state is "no mode active, only Takeoff
  accepted".
Parent(s):     L1-AER-950, L1-AER-930
Source:        D69; B3 impact list (C5)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-140; success criteria: from a ground start and from the end of a
  landing, each mode selection other than Takeoff is refused and the mode state stays empty with the
  off value of the axis references.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-150

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-150
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Takeoff is selected while the vehicle is on the ground with fuel remaining, the
  autoflight subsystem shall make Takeoff the active both-axes mode and Hover the armed mode.
Rationale:     Takeoff (D69) is engaged on the ground and hands over to Hover (CONOPS §4.4). With no
  fuel the effectors cannot lift the vehicle, so the selection is refused rather than annunciated
  and never completed (C5-Q03, SC-130 zero-fuel variation). A low-fuel state does not refuse it:
  LOW FUEL is the crew's alert.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-960): with L2-AER-AF-250, -660 and -720, the takeoff is selected, flown and
  handed over.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-960
Source:        D69; CONOPS SC-130
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-150; success criteria: in a ground start with fuel, TAKEOFF gives the
  mode state Takeoff active and Hover armed at the next control frame; with fuel exhausted it is
  refused.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
  the active both-axes mode.

  height over a known reference position, so the descent is short and vertical. In any other state
  the selection is refused (rows DT-03, DT-04, DT-06, DT-18, DT-25), and the crew reaches Hover by
  Go to hover.
```

### L2-AER-AF-170

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-170
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Go to hover is selected while the vehicle is airborne and Takeoff is not active,
  the autoflight subsystem shall make Go to hover the active both-axes mode and Hover the armed mode.
Rationale:     Go to hover takes the craft from whatever it is flying (D56) and arms Hover. It is also
  the go-around from Land (D70, SC-100 from below): the vehicle climbs back to the hover height. It
  is refused in Takeoff, which already ends in Hover (C5-Q02), and on the ground (rule DT-03).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the selection side of Go to hover; the manoeuvre is L2-AER-AF-630 and
  the handover L2-AER-AF-240.

  Takeoff gives Go to hover active and Hover armed at the next control frame, including during
```

### L2-AER-AF-190

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-190
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a horizontal mode or a vertical mode is selected while Hover or Go to hover is
  active, the autoflight subsystem shall make the selected mode active in its group and make Altitude
  hold, for a horizontal mode, or Stationary, for a vertical mode, active in the other group.
Rationale:     The crew leaves Hover by selecting a mode of one group, because the panel has no Hover
  selection (D71, IN-C8-09). The other group then needs a mode, and the one that continues what
  Hover was doing is its hold mode: Altitude hold at the altitude the vehicle has, Stationary at the
  reference position it was holding (rules TG-5, TG-6, PR-1). This reproduces SC-110 legs 2 and 8.
  The alternative, refusing the selection until both groups are chosen, makes a one-step scripted
  departure impossible (C5-Q01).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with L2-AER-AF-180, every selection of a group mode has a defined
  result in every airborne state.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D71; CONOPS SC-110 legs 2 and 8; C5-Q01
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-190; success criteria: from Hover and from Go to hover, each of STAT,
  HDG, ALT, GO ALT and V/S gives the state of rows DT-12 to DT-16, and the horizontal position and
  the altitude have no step at the transition.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-200

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-200
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Takeoff is active, the autoflight subsystem shall refuse each mode selection
  other than Takeoff.
Rationale:     The vehicle is on the ground or below the operating volume with the gear extended or in
  transit, and Takeoff already ends in Hover (CONOPS §4.4). Letting the crew redirect it there could
  leave it at a low height in a mode that holds a height only at a tolerance that the gear
  clearance does not allow (C5-Q02). The crew can leave Hover once the handover is made.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-960): the takeoff runs to its handover without interruption.
```

### L2-AER-AF-230

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-230
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the capture condition of AF_MODE_LOGIC for the armed Altitude hold is met, the
  autoflight subsystem shall make Altitude hold the active vertical mode, with its target, and clear
  the armed mode.
Rationale:     The capture is the engagement of the armed Altitude hold (glossary: capture). The
  condition is that the altitude still to go is within the tolerance [P-AF-110], or is at most the
  distance in which the vertical speed reference stops at the vertical acceleration [P-AF-230]
  (AF_MODE_LOGIC §5; DD-C5-03); the onset of the level-off then needs no separate selection. The
  target is the selected altitude at the frame of the capture (L2-AER-AF-350 for the other cases).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the capture is the automatic transition Go to altitude to Altitude hold
  and Vertical speed to Altitude hold of the catalogue.

Source:        D16, D56; DD-C5-03; CONOPS SC-020, SC-040
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed

  mode state changes from Go to altitude or Vertical speed with Altitude hold armed to Altitude hold
  active with nothing armed at the frame where the condition of AF_MODE_LOGIC first holds, and not
```

### L2-AER-AF-250

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-250
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the vehicle is stationary at the hover height with the landing gear retracted
  while Takeoff is active, the autoflight subsystem shall make Hover the active both-axes mode and
  clear the armed mode.
Rationale:     Takeoff hands over to Hover once the gear is retracted (CONOPS §4.4, "C5 confirms"): the
  retraction is commanded at the hover height (L2-AER-AF-720) and takes the gear transit time
  [P-DYN-050]; Hover starts when it is done, so the vehicle never holds with the gear in transit
  without Takeoff still being annunciated.
```

### L2-AER-AF-260

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-260
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall judge the vehicle stationary at the hover height only
  while the height above the ground is within [P-AF-110] of [P-AF-210], the horizontal speed is at
  most [P-AF-170] and the magnitude of the vertical speed is at most [P-AF-090].
Rationale:     "Stationary at the hover height" is the engagement condition of Hover (D56) and needs a
  definition (glossary proposal). The three tolerances are the altitude hold tolerance, the hover
  horizontal speed tolerance and the vertical speed tolerance already in the database; no new
  parameter. [P-AF-170] is at most the landing envelope horizontal speed [P-SYS-510], so that Land
  from Hover meets the envelope (L1-AER-970).
```

### L2-AER-AF-270

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-270
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the gear state shows the vehicle on the ground while Land is active, the
  autoflight subsystem shall end Land and leave no mode active and no mode armed in the same control
  frame.
Rationale:     A landing ends the descent (D69): the contact is detected from the gear state, the
  effectors are switched off through the off value (L2-AER-AF-410), and the mode state is empty, so
  that nothing is annunciated but the gear (SC-130 step 5, C5-Q04). The same control frame as the
  detection keeps the AF share of the effectors-off latency [P-SYS-480] at one frame.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-970, -930): with L2-AER-AF-410, the landing ends in the on-ground state of
  D69.
```

### L2-AER-AF-300

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-300
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall evaluate the automatic transitions of AF_MODE_LOGIC once
  at each control frame, after it has decided the mode selections of that frame.
Rationale:     A fixed order makes a selection and a capture in one frame give one result (for
  example GO ALT with a selected altitude equal to the present one captures at once) and keeps the
  runs reproducible (L2-AER-AF-080). The transitions do not chain (AF_MODE_LOGIC §4), so one
  evaluation is complete. The fuel exhaustion and the end of Land are evaluated before the
  selections (TR-01, TR-02).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): ties the selection rows and the transition rows into one frame
  sequence.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
```

### L2-AER-AF-310

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-310
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the target of a selector changes, the autoflight subsystem shall guide toward the
  new target only in a mode that uses that selector, without changing the mode state.
Rationale:     "Changing a target does not change the mode. The vehicle goes to the new target"
  (CONOPS §4.4, SC-020 step 3, SC-050 steps 1 and 2). A selector that no active or armed mode uses has
  no effect: in particular a new selected altitude does not move an Altitude hold that is already
  active, whose target is its own (rules TG-1, TG-4 of AF_MODE_LOGIC). The selectors and the modes
  that use them are the table of AF_MODE_LOGIC §9.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with L2-AER-AF-320, -350 and -520 to -530, each selected quantity is
  followed by the mode that uses it.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS §4.4, SC-020, SC-050
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-310; success criteria: a change of the selected speed, heading, altitude
  or vertical speed during a mode that uses it changes the corresponding reference and not the mode
  state; a change of the selected altitude during Altitude hold changes neither.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-320

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-320
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Go to altitude is active, the autoflight subsystem shall command a vertical
  speed toward the selected altitude whose magnitude is [P-AF-220], or that of the selected vertical
  speed once the panel has accepted one since Go to altitude was selected.
Rationale:     Go to altitude climbs or descends at the standard vertical speed, "which the crew can
  change" (D56, D70). The standard speed is not a selector value, so the panel holds whatever the
  crew last selected and AF decides which speed to fly from the accepted set of the frame
  (IN-C8-03). Go to altitude always starts at the standard speed; a selected vertical speed accepted
  in the frame of the selection, or later, replaces it. The direction is always toward the selected
  altitude, so only the magnitude of the selected vertical speed is used, and a selected vertical
  speed of zero is ignored (C5-Q08). The speeds up to [P-SYS-240] and [P-SYS-250] that the panel
  accepts (L2-AER-FCP-130) are the climb and descent capability of L1-AER-320 and -330.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-320, -330): with L2-AER-AF-600 (the vertical speed is held), L2-AER-AF-620 and
  -430 (vertical speed in Vertical speed mode and the ramp), the climb and descent speeds up to the
  limits are commanded in both vertical modes that climb or descend.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-320, L1-AER-330
Source:        D56, D70; IN-C8-03; CONOPS SC-020
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-320; success criteria: in SC-020 and its climb variation, the vertical
  speed reference has the magnitude [P-AF-220] until a selected vertical speed is accepted and the
  magnitude of that selection from then on, toward the selected altitude, and a re-selection of GO
  ALT restarts at [P-AF-220].
Parameters:    P-AF-220, P-SYS-240, P-SYS-250
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-240, P-SYS-250 values TBR (TT-010), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-330

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-330
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a mode that holds the horizontal position becomes active from a mode that does
  not, the autoflight subsystem shall set the reference position to the position at which the
  horizontal velocity reaches zero under the horizontal acceleration limit.
Rationale:     What "the position held" is when a hold is entered with the vehicle moving (SC-090 TBR,
  #78): the position at engagement cannot be held, since the vehicle is moving; the position at which
  it stops can. The modes that hold the horizontal position are Stationary, Go to hover, Hover,
  Takeoff, Land and Heading at a selected speed of zero. From the ground the velocity is zero, so the
  reference is the ground position (L1-AER-960). The limit is the part of [P-AF-200] that
  [P-AF-230] leaves (DD-C5-07, AF_MODE_LOGIC §11); the stopping position is the horizontal position
  plus the velocity times its magnitude over twice that limit (DD-C5-04, C5-Q05).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-460): with L2-AER-AF-340 and -510, the reference position is defined for every
  entry to a hold, and its accuracy is the share [P-AF-160].
Parent(s):     L1-AER-460
Source:        #78, D16, D56; DD-C5-04; CONOPS SC-090
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-AF-330; success criteria: for Stationary engaged from Heading at
  several speeds and directions, the reference position equals the position and velocity of the
  engagement frame propagated at the horizontal acceleration limit to zero velocity, within
  [P-AF-160].
Parameters:    P-AF-200, P-AF-230, P-AF-160
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-200 value TBR (TT-040), P-AF-160 value TBR (TT-050), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-340

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-340
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a mode that holds the horizontal position becomes active from a mode that holds
  it, the autoflight subsystem shall keep the reference position unchanged.
Rationale:     The hold modes hand over to one another without a new capture: Go to hover to Hover,
  Takeoff to Hover, Hover to Land, Hover to Stationary (DT-12, TG-6). Re-capturing at each handover
  would let the reference creep by the tracking error at every handover, and the budget [P-SYS-450]
  counts from the start of the hold, not from each handover (PR-4).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-460): with L2-AER-AF-330, every hold has one reference position from its start.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-460
Source:        D56, D60; DD-C5-04
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-340; success criteria: at each handover between modes that hold the
  horizontal position in SC-100, SC-130 and SC-110, the reference position is the same before and
  after, and the horizontal position stays within [P-SYS-450] of it from [P-SYS-460] after the start
  of the hold.
Parameters:    P-SYS-450, P-SYS-460
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-450 value TBR (TT-050), P-SYS-460 value TBR (TT-060), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-350

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-350
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Altitude hold becomes active other than by the capture of the selected altitude,
  the autoflight subsystem shall set its target to the altitude at which the vertical speed reference
  reaches zero under [P-AF-230].
Rationale:     SC-050 step 5 TBR: with Altitude hold selected during a climb, the vehicle "holds the
  altitude reached". The target is the altitude where the vertical speed reference stops at the
  vertical acceleration (the level-off altitude, DD-C5-03): a target at the altitude of the selection
  would need an instantaneous stop and a return (C5-Q06). It applies to ALT selected directly, to
  the group selections over Hover and Go to hover (rule TG-5) and to the start of an airborne initial
  mode state. At zero vertical speed it is the present altitude.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with L2-AER-AF-230, every Altitude hold has a defined target.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-050; DD-C5-03
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-350; success criteria: with ALT selected during a climb and during a
  descent at several vertical speeds, the Altitude hold target recorded is the altitude of the
  selection frame plus the vertical speed reference times its magnitude over twice [P-AF-230], and
  the vertical speed reference reaches zero there.
Parameters:    P-AF-230
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-230 value TBR (TT-830), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-360

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-360
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Vertical speed is active and the selected altitude is not ahead in the direction
  of the selected vertical speed, the autoflight subsystem shall make Altitude hold the active
  vertical mode.
Rationale:     SC-040 TBR ("a selected altitude behind the direction of motion"). Altitude hold cannot
  capture an altitude the vehicle is moving away from, so a Vertical speed whose selected altitude is
  behind, or whose selected vertical speed is zero, ends at once in Altitude hold, by the same
  capture mechanism as the normal one (AF_MODE_LOGIC §5). Refusing the selection instead would need a
  second rule and a refusal that the crew sees only as a mode that did not engage (C5-Q06).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with L2-AER-AF-230, every Vertical speed ends in Altitude hold.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-040 TBR; C5-Q06
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-360; success criteria: V/S selected with the selected altitude below the
  vehicle and a positive selected vertical speed, with the selected altitude above it and a negative
  one, and with a zero selected vertical speed, each gives Altitude hold active at the frame of the
  selection and no Vertical speed.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-400

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-400
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While autoflight is engaged, the autoflight subsystem shall give a pitch angle
  reference of zero and a roll angle reference of zero.
Rationale:     Pitch and roll are not used for now (D57, revising D10): the vehicle translates along
  three axes and rotates about the vertical axis only, in every mode. This is a rule for every mode
  and is stated once. AF gives the references; FC holds the angles within [P-AF-180] of them
  (L1-AER-390).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-390): the AF child gives the level references, and the FC child holds the
  attitude to them; together they keep the angles within [P-AF-180] of zero.

Source:        D57; N-160
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-400; success criteria: in every mode of every test run while engaged,
  the recorded pitch angle reference and roll angle reference are zero.
Parameters:    P-AF-180
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-180 value TBR (TT-190), Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin

Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-410

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-410
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While autoflight is not engaged, the autoflight subsystem shall give flight control the
  off value of the axis references, from the control frame in which autoflight ceased to be engaged.
Rationale:     On the ground the effectors are off so that no fuel is used (D69, L1-AER-930); after
  fuel exhaustion they produce nothing in any case (D68). The off value replaces the six references
  and carries no mode information (L1-AER-420); FC commands zero effector output and sets the
  effector activation state off while it is given (IN-C3-26, IN-C3-30). Giving it in the frame in
  which the mode state became empty keeps the AF share of [P-SYS-480] at one control frame. The
  references leave the off value in the frame in which Takeoff or an airborne initial mode state makes
  autoflight engaged (L2-AER-AF-760).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-930, -950): AF sets the off reference, FC commands zero and EF produces zero.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-930, L1-AER-950, L1-AER-420
Source:        D69; CONOPS SC-130; IN-C3-26
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-410; success criteria: in a ground start, after each landing and after
  fuel exhaustion, the axis references recorded take the off value at the control frame in which the
  mode state becomes empty and keep it until a mode becomes active.
Parameters:    P-SYS-480
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-480 value TBR (TT-070), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-420

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-420
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall keep the magnitude of the acceleration of its
  translational velocity reference at most [P-AF-200].
Rationale:     Manoeuvres are g-limited (D16): this is the autoflight share of the vehicle acceleration
  budget, [P-AF-200] plus the flight-control allowance [P-FC-020] at most [P-SYS-440]
  (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2, L1-AER-300), which in turn keeps the sensed acceleration under
  [P-SYS-120] (L1-AER-290). The acceleration is that of the reference in three dimensions: the
  horizontal part, a turning velocity reference included, and the vertical part (DD-C5-07 splits
  [P-AF-200] into a horizontal limit and the vertical one [P-AF-230]).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-300, -290): the AF share is bounded; FC's child bounds its transients.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-300, L1-AER-290
Source:        D16, D38; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.2; DD-C5-07
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-420; success criteria: in every test run, the magnitude of the change of
  the recorded translational velocity reference per control frame, divided by the frame period, is
  at most [P-AF-200], in every mode and at every transition, in winds within the wind envelope.
Parameters:    P-AF-200, P-FC-020, P-SYS-440, P-SYS-120
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-200 value TBR (TT-040), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-430
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall change its vertical speed reference at a rate of at most
  [P-AF-230] in magnitude.
Rationale:     The vertical speed ramps rather than steps (D70): about ten seconds to reach the
  standard speed, and a level-off over about 170 ft. The same limit applies to an engagement, a new
  selection, a reversal and a level-off (P-AF-230 description). It is the vertical component of the
  manoeuvre acceleration, so the horizontal limit is derived from it (DD-C5-07).
```

### L2-AER-AF-440

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-440
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall keep the magnitude of its heading rate reference at most
  [P-SYS-270] and at most the quotient of [P-AF-200] by its horizontal speed reference.
Rationale:     Owner ruling D72 Q1: [P-SYS-230] is a ground speed, and the lateral acceleration of a
  turn is the horizontal speed times the heading rate (option T1, D64). C5 bounds the commanded
  heading rate so that this product stays within [P-AF-200]. At the highest speed the quotient is a
  little above [P-SYS-270], which governs there; at lower speeds up to the stated rate is reached.
  The rate is also the heading rate capability of L1-AER-350. The bound is on the reference, so a
  tailwind or an overshoot that raises the actual ground speed is FC's transient allowance
  [P-FC-020].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-350, -300): AF commands rates up to [P-SYS-270] where the acceleration budget
  allows, and the budget is closed by the second bound.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-350, L1-AER-300
Source:        D64, D72 (Q1); IN-C1-32; IN-C6-12; DD-C5-07
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-440; success criteria: in heading changes at selected horizontal speeds
  from zero to [P-SYS-230], the recorded heading rate reference never exceeds [P-SYS-270], and its
  product with the recorded horizontal speed reference never exceeds [P-AF-200]; at zero speed the
  rate reaches [P-SYS-270].
Parameters:    P-SYS-270, P-AF-200, P-SYS-230, P-FC-020
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-270 value TBR (TT-010), P-AF-200 value TBR (TT-040), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-450

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-450
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The autoflight subsystem shall change the heading reference only while Heading is
  active.
Rationale:     The heading behaviour of each mode (CONOPS §4.4, D57): Heading turns to the selected
  heading; in Stationary, Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff and Land the heading is held at its value when
  the first of these modes became active, and the vertical modes do not change the heading (rules
  HD-1 to HD-3). The value at the first activation is the reference in effect, or the navigation
  heading when there was none (L2-AER-AF-920).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): with the Heading records, the heading behaviour in each mode is stated.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D57; CONOPS §4.4; A2-Q03
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-450; success criteria: in SC-110 and SC-050, the recorded heading
  reference changes only at frames where Heading is active.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-500
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Stationary is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give a horizontal velocity
  reference that brings the horizontal velocity to zero and a reference position that it then holds.
Rationale:     Stationary stops the horizontal motion within the acceleration limits (D16) and holds
  the position over the ground, with the effectors fighting the wind (D56, D17). The reference
  position is the stopping position (L2-AER-AF-330); the heading is held (L2-AER-AF-450); the vertical
  mode is independent. This is the reference mode; the engagement, capture shape and flow-down are
  the Phase 4 section M0.

  L2-AER-AF-510.

  initial horizontal speeds, the horizontal velocity reference reaches zero and the reference
```
