### L2-AER-AF-510

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-510
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While the horizontal position is held, the autoflight subsystem shall give a reference
  position within [P-AF-160] of the target position of the hold at each control frame that follows the
  start of the hold by at least [P-SYS-460].
Rationale:     The AF share of the position-holding budget (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.4, L1-AER-460): the
  reference position versus the target, through the capture and the hold, with [P-FC-010] for the
  regulation of the vehicle to the reference. The target position is the stopping position or the one
  carried over (L2-AER-AF-330, -340). The hold starts as rule PR-4 of AF_MODE_LOGIC says, and the
  time [P-SYS-460] covers the deceleration from [P-SYS-230] (about 16 s) and the capture (D70). It
  holds in Stationary, Hover, Takeoff, Land and Heading at zero speed, and, with the position error
  as a horizontal error only (D60).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-460, -960): with the FC and EF children, [P-AF-160] plus [P-FC-010] is at most
  [P-SYS-450]; for Takeoff the target is the ground position.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-460, L1-AER-960
Source:        D60; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.4
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-AF-510; success criteria: from the recorded position and velocity of
  the first frame of each hold, and the recorded reference position, the distance between the
  reference position and the target position is at most [P-AF-160] at every frame from [P-SYS-460]
  after the start, in Stationary, Hover, Takeoff, Land and Heading at zero speed.
Parameters:    P-AF-160, P-SYS-460, P-SYS-450, P-FC-010
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-160, P-SYS-450, P-FC-010 values TBR (TT-050); P-SYS-460 value TBR (TT-060),
  Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-520

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-520
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Heading is active, the autoflight subsystem shall change the heading reference
  toward the selected heading, through the smaller angle and at the largest heading rate that the
  bound on the heading rate reference allows.
Rationale:     Heading turns the nose to the selected heading (D56), by the shorter way ("mode logic to
  confirm", SC-050 step 2; on an exact half turn clockwise, HD-1). The largest permitted rate gives
  the heading rate capability up to [P-SYS-270] (L1-AER-350) where the speed allows; at zero speed
  it turns the vehicle in place (SC-120).

Criticality:   Not assessed

  to the selected heading through the smaller angle at the heading rate of L2-AER-AF-440, in both
  senses and across the north direction.
Parameters:    P-SYS-270
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-270 value TBR (TT-010), Martin, G2

Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-530

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-530
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Heading is active with a non-zero selected horizontal speed, the autoflight
  subsystem shall give a horizontal velocity reference along the heading reference whose magnitude
  goes to the selected horizontal speed within the acceleration limit.
Rationale:     In Heading the vehicle moves along its heading at the selected horizontal speed, a
  ground speed (D56, D72): "its motion turns with" the heading (SC-050 step 2). The velocity
  direction follows the heading reference, not the final heading, so a turn bends the path. The
  selected speed runs from zero to [P-SYS-230], the capability of L1-AER-310. A speed change and a
  turn share the horizontal limit (AF_MODE_LOGIC §11, DD-C5-07). The track follows the heading
  (CV-550 reading, SC-060); its tolerance [P-AF-140] is for the mode section M5.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-310): the horizontal speeds up to [P-SYS-230] are commanded in Heading; the
  vehicle sustains them by the FC and EF children.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-310, L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D72; CONOPS SC-060
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-530; success criteria: in SC-060 and its wind variation, at selected
  speeds up to and including [P-SYS-230], the horizontal velocity reference has the direction of the
  heading reference and its magnitude reaches the selected speed with the acceleration of
  L2-AER-AF-420.
Parameters:    P-SYS-230, P-AF-140
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-140 value TBD (TT-180), Martin, G3 (M5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-540

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-540
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Heading is active with a selected horizontal speed of zero, the autoflight
  subsystem shall hold the reference position.
Rationale:     SC-120 TBR: with no horizontal velocity a heading change turns the craft in place
  (D56). Regulating a zero velocity alone would let the vehicle drift with the integral of the
  velocity error in a wind, so that a turn in place would move it. Holding the reference position, as
  Stationary does, keeps it where it is, and Heading differs from Stationary only by the turn
  (C5-Q07). The reference position is acquired as in L2-AER-AF-330.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the zero-speed case of the Heading mode.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-120 TBR; C5-Q07
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-540; success criteria: in SC-120 with a steady wind and gusts, the
  horizontal position stays within [P-SYS-450] of its value at the start from [P-SYS-460] on, and the
  heading goes to the selected heading.
Parameters:    P-SYS-450, P-SYS-460
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-450 value TBR (TT-050); P-SYS-460 value TBR (TT-060), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-560

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-560
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Altitude hold is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give an altitude
  reference equal to its target and a vertical speed reference of zero.
Rationale:     Altitude hold holds the altitude (D56): the captured selected altitude, or the level-off
  altitude of a direct selection (L2-AER-AF-230, -350). The reference approaches the target without
  passing it, within the vertical acceleration limit (L2-AER-AF-430); the vehicle overshoot is
  L2-AER-AF-590 and the hold tolerance L2-AER-AF-580. The mode section M3 refines the capture.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the mode record of Altitude hold.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-020, SC-040, SC-050
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-560; success criteria: in SC-020, SC-040 and SC-050, once Altitude hold
  is active the recorded altitude reference equals the Altitude hold target and the vertical speed
  reference is zero.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-580

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-580
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While Altitude hold or Hover is active, the autoflight subsystem shall hold the
  altitude within [P-AF-110] of its target from [P-AF-190] after the altitude reference reaches it.
Rationale:     The altitude error is a separate tolerance from the horizontal position budget (D60,
  L1-AER-460). In Hover with the gear extended (after a go-around, or before the retraction of a
  takeoff) the error must stay inside the gear clearance, [P-AF-210] minus [P-DYN-040], with the
  tilt allowed by [P-AF-180] (IN-C1-44; the proposed values meet it, `param_proposals.md`). The
  value is shared between the AF reference and the FC regulation by the mode sections (M0, M3, M8,
  FC1). The tolerance is on the altitude of the centre of gravity; the hover target is the hover
  height above the body plus half of [P-DYN-030].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the vehicle-level tolerance of the two altitude-holding modes.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D60, D70; IN-C1-44
Allocated to:  FC, EF
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-580; success criteria: in SC-020, SC-100, SC-110 and SC-130 with gusts
  within the wind envelope, the altitude is within [P-AF-110] of the target at every output sample
  from [P-AF-190] after the altitude reference reaches the target; in Hover with the gear extended
  the margin to the ground contact stays positive.
Parameters:    P-AF-110, P-AF-190, P-AF-210, P-DYN-040, P-AF-180
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-110, P-AF-190 values TBR (TT-180); P-AF-180 value TBR (TT-190), Martin, G2/G3
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-590

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-590
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     When Altitude hold captures the selected altitude, the autoflight subsystem shall keep
  the altitude from passing the selected altitude by more than [P-AF-100].
Rationale:     The level-off ends on the selected altitude "without passing it by more than
  [P-AF-100]" (CONOPS SC-020 step 4). The reference does not pass the target (L2-AER-AF-560), so the
  overshoot is the tracking overshoot of the vehicle; the tolerance is shared by the mode sections.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the capture accuracy of the two modes that capture.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-020, SC-040
Allocated to:  FC, EF
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-590; success criteria: in SC-020, SC-040 and their variations, climbing
  and descending at the standard and at the highest selectable vertical speed, the altitude does not
  pass the selected altitude by more than [P-AF-100].
Parameters:    P-AF-100
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-100 value TBR (TT-180), Martin, G3
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-600

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-600
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While Vertical speed is active, or Go to altitude is active, the autoflight subsystem
  shall hold the vertical speed within [P-AF-090] of its commanded vertical speed from [P-AF-190] after
  the vertical speed reference reaches it.
Rationale:     The tracking tolerance of the selected vertical speed (L1-AER-320, -330 rationale; CONOPS
  SC-020, SC-040): "Settled" means within the tolerance, reached within a settling time after the
  reference reaches the target (the definition of [P-AF-190] proposed in
  `param_proposals.md`). The vehicle-level tolerance is shared by the AF reference and the FC regulation
  by the mode sections. Together with L2-AER-AF-320, -430 and -620, the climb and descent speeds up
  to [P-SYS-240] and [P-SYS-250] are sustained in both vertical modes.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-320, -330): the vertical speed tolerance that the two L1 records leave to L2.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-320, L1-AER-330
Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-020, SC-040
Allocated to:  FC, EF
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-600; success criteria: in SC-020 and SC-040 and their variations, at the
  standard vertical speed, at the highest climb and descent speed, and at zero and highest horizontal
  speed, the vertical speed is within [P-AF-090] of the commanded one at every output sample from
  [P-AF-190] after the reference reaches it.
Parameters:    P-AF-090, P-AF-190, P-SYS-240, P-SYS-250
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-090, P-AF-190 values TBR (TT-180), Martin, G3
Owner:         Martin

Version:       1
```

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-620
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Vertical speed is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give a vertical speed
  reference equal to the selected vertical speed.
Rationale:     Vertical speed climbs or descends at the selected constant rate (D56); the selected rate
  is signed, positive up, and runs from minus [P-SYS-250] to [P-SYS-240] (L2-AER-FCP-130), the climb
  and descent capability of L1-AER-320 and -330. The reference reaches the rate by the ramp of
  L2-AER-AF-430. A change of the selected rate during the mode retargets the rate (L2-AER-AF-310).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-320, -330): the mode record of Vertical speed; the end of the climb is the
  capture (L2-AER-AF-230).

  speed reference equals the selected vertical speed, up to [P-SYS-240] and down to minus [P-SYS-250],
```

### L2-AER-AF-630

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-630
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Go to hover is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give references that bring
  the vehicle to zero velocity on each axis at the hover height above the ground, from above and from
  below.
Rationale:     Go to hover takes the craft from whatever it is flying, stops the horizontal motion and
  goes to the hover height, descending from above or climbing from below (D56, D70). Horizontally it
  stops as Stationary does (reference position as L2-AER-AF-330); vertically it flies to the hover
  altitude at the standard vertical speed [P-AF-220] with the ramps of L2-AER-AF-430, and the selected
  altitude and vertical speed are not used. The heading is held. Hover is armed (L2-AER-AF-170) and
  engages by L2-AER-AF-240.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): the mode record of Go to hover.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        D56, D70; CONOPS SC-100
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-630; success criteria: in SC-100 from above at the highest horizontal
  speed, and from below as a go-around in SC-130, the horizontal and vertical velocity references go
  to zero and the vertical position reference to the hover height, with the accelerations of
  L2-AER-AF-420 and -430.
Parameters:    P-AF-210, P-AF-220
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-640

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-640
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Hover is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give references that hold the
  hover height above the ground and the reference position.
Rationale:     Hover holds the height and the position over the ground against the wind (D56). The
  reference position is the one carried over (L2-AER-AF-340), the heading is held, and the references
  are constant in still air, so that the fuel used in a hold is that of the weight alone (the
  premise of the endurance budget L1-AER-360 and L2-AER-FU-290).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450, -460): the mode record of Hover; the accuracy is L2-AER-AF-510 and -580.

Source:        D56; CONOPS SC-100, SC-110 leg 1
Allocated to:  AF, FC
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-640; success criteria: while Hover is active in still air, the recorded
  position and altitude references and the heading reference are constant, and the velocity references
  are zero.
Parameters:    P-AF-210
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-650

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-650
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     While Hover is active, the autoflight subsystem shall keep the horizontal speed at most
  [P-AF-170].
Rationale:     L1-AER-970 leaves the Hover speed tolerance to C5: a Land from Hover must meet the
  landing envelope horizontal speed [P-SYS-510], so [P-AF-170] is at most [P-SYS-510]; the proposed
  value is half of it, for the gust response at the touchdown (`param_proposals.md`). The tolerance
  is on the ground speed. Land holds the Hover reference position through the contact
  (L2-AER-AF-670), so the speed at the contact is that of Hover.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-970): with L2-AER-AF-670 and -710, the horizontal speed condition of the
  landing envelope is met.

Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-650; success criteria: in SC-130 and SC-100, with and without wind
  within the wind envelope, the horizontal speed is at most [P-AF-170] at every output sample in
  Hover and at the contact of the landing, and [P-AF-170] is at most [P-SYS-510].
```

### L2-AER-AF-660

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-660
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Takeoff is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give references that climb the
  vehicle vertically at [P-AF-240] to the hover height above the ground over its ground position,
  holding the heading.
Rationale:     Takeoff (D69) powers up, climbs at [P-AF-240] to the hover height over the current
  position, retracts the gear and hands over to Hover. The reference is a vertical speed with the
  ramp of L2-AER-AF-430 until the hover height is near, then the hover altitude (AF_MODE_LOGIC §13,
  rule TO-1); the horizontal reference holds the ground position (PR-3); the heading is held at its
  value at the engagement. Flight control keeps the vehicle on the ground until the upward force
  exceeds the weight (liftoff, IN-C1-43); the wind limit of the takeoff is [P-SYS-580].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-960): with L2-AER-AF-150, -250, -510 and -720, the takeoff is selected, flown
  to the hover height with the gear retracted, within [P-SYS-450] of the ground position, and handed
  to Hover.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-960
Source:        D69, D70, D73; CONOPS SC-130
Allocated to:  AF, FC, EF
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-660; success criteria: in SC-130 and SC-110 leg 0, at full fuel and near
  dry mass, in still air and in a steady wind of [P-SYS-580] from several directions, the vehicle
  reaches the hover height with the gear retracted, and its horizontal position stays within
  [P-SYS-450] of the ground position.
Parameters:    P-AF-240, P-AF-210, P-SYS-580, P-SYS-450
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-240 value TBR (TT-830), P-SYS-580 value TBR (TT-860), P-SYS-450 value TBR (TT-050),
  Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-670

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-670
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Land is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give references that descend the
  vehicle vertically at [P-AF-240] over the reference position, holding the heading, until it gives
  the off value.
Rationale:     Land (D69) descends at [P-AF-240] from the hover height to the touchdown, well inside the
  vertical speed limit of the landing envelope [P-SYS-500]. The downward reference stays until the
  off value is given, so that flight control commands no more than the weight at the contact and the
  vehicle does not lift off again (IN-C1-43, MS-DYN liftoff rule). The descent starts only with the
  gear extended (L2-AER-AF-710). The reference position is that of Hover (PR-3), so the horizontal
  speed at the contact is that of Hover (L2-AER-AF-650).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-970): with L2-AER-AF-160, -270, -650, -700 and -710, a landing from Hover is
  made on the extended gear within the landing envelope, at a vertical speed below [P-SYS-500].
Parent(s):     L1-AER-970
Source:        D53, D69, D70; CONOPS SC-130
Allocated to:  AF, FC, EF
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-670; success criteria: in SC-130 and SC-110 leg 7a at full fuel and near
  dry mass, in still air and in a steady wind of [P-SYS-430] with gusts of [P-SYS-560] from several
  directions, each landing from Hover gives one landing event, no crash, and the vertical speed at
  the contact is at most [P-SYS-500].
Parameters:    P-AF-240, P-SYS-500, P-SYS-430, P-SYS-560
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-240 value TBR (TT-830), P-SYS-500 value TBR (TT-090), P-SYS-430, P-SYS-560 values
  TBR (TT-030), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-700

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-700
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Land becomes active, the autoflight subsystem shall command the landing gear to
  extend.
Rationale:     Land extends the gear before the descent (D69, L1-AER-910). The command is issued in
  the frame in which Land becomes active, so the gear is in transit for at most the transit time
  [P-DYN-050] before the descent can start (L2-AER-AF-710). If the gear is already extended, as after
  a go-around, the command changes nothing (rule GR-3).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-910, -970): AF commands the extension, DYN moves the gear (L2-AER-DYN-270),
  and AF descends only when it is extended.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-910, L1-AER-970
Source:        D69; CONOPS SC-130
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-700; success criteria: in SC-130 step 4 the recorded landing gear
  command changes to extend at the control frame in which Land becomes active.
Parameters:    P-DYN-050
TBD/TBR:       P-DYN-050 value TBR (TT-850), Martin, G2 (C1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-710

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-710
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While Land is active, the autoflight subsystem shall give a downward vertical speed
  reference only while the gear state shows the gear extended.
Rationale:     A ground contact with the gear in transit or retracted is a crash whatever the speed
  (L1-AER-270, D73 B3-3), so autoflight descends below the hover height only with the gear extended
  (L1-AER-910 rationale, IN-C1-42). Until then Land holds the hover height and the reference
  position.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-910, -970): the other half of L2-AER-AF-700.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-910, L1-AER-970
Source:        D69, D73; IN-C1-42
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-710; success criteria: in SC-130 step 4 and with the gear starting
  retracted, the vertical speed reference is not downward until the gear state shows the gear
  extended, and the vehicle makes no ground contact with the gear in transit.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-720

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-720
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Takeoff has brought the vehicle to the hover height, the autoflight subsystem
  shall command the landing gear to retract.
Rationale:     Takeoff retracts the gear once the vehicle is at the hover height (D69, L1-AER-910).
  The condition is the height above the ground within [P-AF-110] of [P-AF-210] or above it while the
  vehicle is airborne (rule GR-2), so the gear is never retracted on the ground. Retracting at the
  hover height, not earlier, keeps the gear under the vehicle through the liftoff.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-910, -960): AF commands the retraction, DYN moves the gear, and Hover starts
  when it is retracted (L2-AER-AF-250).
Parent(s):     L1-AER-910, L1-AER-960
Source:        D69; CONOPS SC-130 step 2
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-720; success criteria: in SC-130 step 2 the recorded landing gear
  command changes to retract at the first control frame at which the vehicle is airborne and its
  height above the ground is within [P-AF-110] of [P-AF-210] or above it, and not before.
Parameters:    P-AF-110, P-AF-210
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-110 value TBR (TT-180), Martin, G2/G3
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-730

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-730
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the gear state shows the vehicle on the ground, the autoflight subsystem shall
  command the landing gear to stay extended.
Rationale:     On the ground the gear stays extended and does not respond to a retraction command,
  because a retraction there would drop the body onto the ground (L1-AER-910, L1-AER-920); a command
  to retract given on the ground would take effect at the first airborne step (IN-C1-41), which is
  after the liftoff and before the hover height. AF therefore commands extension throughout the
  on-ground state, and retracts only by L2-AER-AF-720.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-910): the "never retract on the ground" half of the gear rules.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-910
Source:        D69; IN-C1-41
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-730; success criteria: in SC-130 from the start to the liftoff, in every
  second takeoff and after each landing, the recorded landing gear command is extend.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-740

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-740
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a run starts, the autoflight subsystem shall set the landing gear command to the
  gear position of the initial condition.
Rationale:     Before the first command the commanded position is the initial gear position (IN-C1-41),
  so a run does not begin with a gear movement that the crew did not ask for. A ground start has the
  gear extended (L1-AER-950); an airborne test start may have it retracted (D73).

  the gear retracted, the landing gear command of the first control frame equals the initial gear
  position, and the gear state shows no transit.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-750

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-750
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When a run starts on the ground, the autoflight subsystem shall start with no mode
  active, no mode armed and the off value of the axis references.
Rationale:     A run starts on the ground, on the extended gear, with the effectors off (D69,
  L1-AER-950); the recommendation to C5 was that no mode is active (A2-Q05, #60, TT-610). Takeoff is
  the mode engaged from there. A ground start with a mode in the initial condition is an
  initial-condition error (L2-AER-AF-910).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-950): AF sets the effectors-off reference, FC commands zero and EF produces
  nothing, which is the ground start of D69.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-950, L1-AER-660
Source:        D69; CONOPS §4.4
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-750; success criteria: the first output sample of a ground start shows
  the empty mode state and the off value of the axis references.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Valid initial mode states (#60, TT-610), closed here, Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-760

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-760
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When Takeoff becomes active, the autoflight subsystem shall end the off value of the
  axis references in the same control frame.
Rationale:     "Taking off" is the interval from the engagement of Takeoff to the liftoff, the only
  time the effectors work on the ground (glossary, L1-AER-930). Flight control sets the effector
  activation state on while the references are not off (IN-C3-26), so the effectors power up in the
  frame of the selection and the liftoff follows when the upward force exceeds the weight.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-930, -960): with L2-AER-AF-410, the off value ends exactly at the engagement
  of Takeoff and returns at the end of Land.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-930, L1-AER-960
Source:        D69; CONOPS SC-130 step 1
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-760; success criteria: in SC-130 step 1, the axis references recorded
  are not the off value from the control frame in which Takeoff becomes active.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-800

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-800
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     At each control frame, the autoflight subsystem shall give the flight control panel
  the annunciation content made of the mode state, each target in use with the quantity it applies
  to, and the landing gear position.
Rationale:     AF decides the content (which modes, which targets, the gear position); FCP presents it
  with the labels of AF_MODE_LOGIC §2 and the crew units (D55, L1-AER-470, -880, I-16). The targets in
  use are those of AF_MODE_LOGIC §9, each with its quantity (altitude, vertical speed, horizontal
  speed or heading) in SI, including a target that AF sets for itself, such as the held altitude
  (IN-C8-05, -06). The gear position is the gear state's, passed on (the gear labels are FCP's,
  IN-C8-07). An empty mode state is a valid content.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): with FCP's presentation and L2-AER-AF-810, a change of the mode state, of a
  target or of the gear position reaches the crew.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-470, L1-AER-880
Source:        D55; IN-C8-05, -06
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-800; success criteria: at every control frame of SC-020, SC-050, SC-100,

  in use of AF_MODE_LOGIC §9 and the gear position of the same frame.
```

### L2-AER-AF-810

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-810
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     When the autoflight subsystem receives a mode selection or a selector target, or the
  gear state changes, the autoflight subsystem shall give the annunciation content that results
  within [P-AF-060] of the change.
Rationale:     The AF share of the annunciation latency [P-SYS-470] (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.5):
  [P-AF-060] is at most [P-SYS-470] minus [P-TM-010], and FCP's share is one output sample period.
  A selection taken at the next control frame and processed in that frame takes two frame periods
  at most ([P-SYS-310] each), which is the proposed value of [P-AF-060]. Changes that AF causes
  itself, such as a capture or a handover, are annunciated in the content of the frame in which they
  occur (AF_MODE_LOGIC §4).
```

### L2-AER-AF-860

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-860
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the fuel state indicates fuel exhaustion, the autoflight subsystem shall make no
  mode active and no mode armed in the same control frame.
Rationale:     Derived from the owner ruling D68 (answer to #59): at exhaustion the effectors stop, the
  vehicle falls and its ground contact is a crash; what the annunciations show is C5's, with the
  suggestion of C2 that autoflight disengages (TT-580, C5-Q04). The alternatives are to keep the modes
  engaged and annunciated, which would show a guidance that no longer acts, and to freeze the last
  state. Disengagement shows the truth, FUEL EXHAUSTED comes from FCP (L2-AER-FCP-400), and the off
  value follows (L2-AER-AF-410). There is no recovery and no crew disengage selection. The state is
  reached in the frame in which the fuel state shows exhaustion, before the selections (TR-01).
  Sufficiency: DD-C5-05 is the one decision; with L2-AER-AF-870 the post-exhaustion state is
  fully defined.
Parent(s):     Derived (DD-C5-05)
Source:        D68; TT-580; CONOPS SC-110 leg 11
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-AF-860; success criteria: in SC-110 at exhaustion, and in a ground start
  with no fuel, the mode state is empty with nothing armed at the control frame in which the fuel
  state shows exhaustion, the axis references take the off value, and the annunciation content has
  the empty mode state.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Annunciations after fuel exhaustion (D68, TT-580), closed here, Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-AF-920

```req
ID:            L2-AER-AF-920
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When autoflight first provides references with a mode active, the autoflight subsystem
  shall start its references from the navigation quantities of that control frame.
Rationale:     The first references of a run that starts airborne, and the references of Takeoff from
  the ground, start from the true state, so that no step is demanded of flight control: the
  horizontal position and velocity, the altitude, the vertical speed and the heading (the stopping
  position of L2-AER-AF-330, the level-off altitude of L2-AER-AF-350). The heading is the navigation
  heading even when its validity flag is false, because NAV gives the CV-260 yaw angle while invalid
  (IN-C6-13); nothing in the mode logic depends on the heading validity, and AF does not use the track
  (IN-C6-10).
```

<!-- END SECTION AF -->

<!-- BEGIN SECTION FCP -->
## FCP — Flight control panel

The flight control panel requirements (task C8). The subject of each statement is the flight control panel (FCP), the eighth Aeronef subsystem (D55). It is part of the flight software and runs once per control frame `[P-SYS-310]`, first in the frame, before navigation, autoflight and flight control (DD-010, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2). FCP implements function F-AER-10 (accept the crew selections), F-AER-13 (present the annunciations and indications) and its part of F-AER-14 (the initial selector values) (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §6.1). It owns the crew side of the crew interface and the conversion between crew-facing units and SI, so that only SI values cross into the rest of the Aeronef (CV-760, L1-AER-870, -880).

**The panel content (#79, D71).** The panel is data, not a device. These two lists are what L2-AER-FCP-020 and -040 close; they are the content of the panel.

| Side | Items | Unit (CV-740) | Records |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Crew selections | Eight mode selections: Stationary, Heading, Altitude hold, Go to altitude, Vertical speed, Go to hover, Takeoff, Land (the engage commands of CONOPS §4.3) | — | -020, -080, -090 |
| | Four selectors, each holding one selected value: heading, horizontal speed, altitude (the target altitude), vertical speed (signed, positive when climbing) | deg, kt, ft, ft/min | -100 to -150 |
| Crew interface output | Annunciations: the mode state, the targets, the landing gear position, the fuel alert (LOW FUEL, FUEL EXHAUSTED); the readout of each selector | crew unit of each target and readout | -320 to -350, -390 to -410 |
| | Indications: altitude, vertical speed, horizontal speed, heading, track, remaining fuel | ft, ft/min, kt, deg, deg, lb | -360 to -380 |

There is no Hover selection (Hover is armed by Go to hover and by Takeoff), no autoflight disengage selection and no manual control (L0-CON-180, L1-AER-520; C8-Q04). The selector readouts are annunciations: they show the targets the crew has selected (glossary *Annunciation*, CONOPS §4.3; CR-C8-02).

**What the panel decides, and what it does not.** FCP takes each crew selection, checks each selected value against its selectable range and selection resolution, converts it to its SI target by the exact CV-750 factor, and gives the selections to autoflight (AF) through ICD-FCP. It does not decide whether a mode selection arms, becomes active or is refused: that is the mode logic of AF (C5, AF_MODE_LOGIC), and FCP passes each mode selection on whatever the mode state. On the output side, AF decides the annunciation content (which modes, which targets, the gear position), navigation (NAV) gives the indicated quantities with their validity, and the FU subsystem gives the fuel state; FCP converts them to crew-facing units, rounds them to their display resolution (CV-780) and outputs them at each output sample. The mode labels are AF's (L1-AER-470: the labels are C5's); the fuel alert labels and the gear-position labels are FCP's (IN-C2-43; C8-Q03).

**The horizontal speed selector is a ground speed** (D72 Q1). Its target is a horizontal speed (CV-480), measured over the ground, which Heading flies; it is not an airspeed, and nothing on the panel calls it one (CV-960).

**No device, no format.** L1-AER-510 (no physical control device, no display format) and L1-AER-520 (no direct manual flight control) are leaves at L1, checked directly over the branch; they have no FCP child. The records here state data items, their units, resolutions and rounding, which L1-AER-510 and CV-780 place outside the display format. No record states a layout, a symbol, a colour or a screen. Nothing here is operator-facing text.

**Verification.** Each record is a leaf at L2 (`Allocated to: —`): the next layer would be the implementation of the panel software (D4), so each record is verified directly by its own method, and the L1 parents by roll-up (D40, D58). Procedure IDs follow the record number (IN-, AN-, TP-, DM-FCP-nnn); Phase 5 (V3) writes the procedures. Most tests drive the panel through the crew interface with a selection script and read the crew interface output and the operational data. The tests of the output mapping (-210, -220, -320 to -410) can also run the panel alone, with a test source of annunciation content, navigation quantities and fuel state at the ICD-FCP boundary (IN-C8-40), as EF does with a test command source (IN-C3-33).

**Coverage of the L1 parents.** Every L1 record allocated to FCP (15, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §6.2) has at least one FCP child. L1-AER-310, -320 and -330 (the envelope) are not allocated to FCP, but their rationales make the panel the place where the selectable range ends (L2, C8); CR-C8-01 adds FCP to their allocation, and the selector records trace to them.

| L1 parent | What FCP guarantees, in the parent's terms | FCP children |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L1-AER-310 (with CR-C8-01) | The selectable horizontal speed runs from zero to [P-SYS-230], and the selectable altitude up to [P-SYS-200] | -110, -120 |
| L1-AER-320 (with CR-C8-01) | The selectable climb vertical speed runs up to [P-SYS-240] | -130 |
| L1-AER-330 (with CR-C8-01) | The selectable descent vertical speed runs down to minus [P-SYS-250], and the selectable altitude down to the altitude at which the height above the ground is [P-SYS-210] at level attitude | -120, -130 |
| L1-AER-430 | FCP exchanges with the vehicle model only the fuel state, by its boundary definition | -070 |
| L1-AER-470 | A change of mode state, target or gear position that AF gives, and a change of a selected value, reaches the crew interface output within the FCP share [P-TM-010]; the mode state, targets, gear position and selector readouts are annunciated | -040, -300, -310, -320, -330, -340, -350 |
| L1-AER-480 | Each crew selection is a time-stamped data item of the crew interface, from a closed list, taken at the next control frame, checked and held | -010, -020, -080, -090, -100 to -150 |
| L1-AER-490 | The annunciations, readouts and indications are crew-interface data items, from a closed list, at each output sample, with their validity | -030, -040, -300, -320, -350, -360, -370 |
| L1-AER-500 | Each selected value, readout, annunciated target and indication is in its CV-740 unit | -100 to -130, -210, -220, -230, -350, -380 |
| L1-AER-560 | The remaining fuel mass is indicated in lb at each output sample | -380 |
| L1-AER-570 | LOW FUEL is annunciated while the fuel state indicates low fuel, within the FCP share of [P-SYS-470] | -310, -390, -410 |
| L1-AER-580 | FUEL EXHAUSTED is annunciated while the fuel state indicates fuel exhaustion, within the FCP share of [P-SYS-470] | -310, -400, -410 |
| L1-AER-610 | Each crew selection taken, accepted or not, goes to the operational data with its timestamp, its selected value and its target | -500 |
| L1-AER-620 | The annunciations presented go to the operational data at each output sample | -510 |
| L1-AER-660 | The initial selected values of the initial condition set the selectors; an invalid one is an initial-condition error | -520, -530 |
| L1-AER-680 | The selections are taken once per control frame and given to AF in that frame | -080 |
| L1-AER-690 | The panel outputs depend only on the run's selections, initial values and inputs, in a defined order | -090, -150, -540 |
| L1-AER-870 | Selected values convert to SI by the exact CV-750 factor without rounding; shown values convert from SI by the same factor, then round once | -200, -210, -220 |
| L1-AER-880 | FCP gives AF only the crew selections in SI, and takes only the annunciation content, the navigation quantities and the fuel state | -050, -060 |

No record is Derived. The design choices inside the records (the intake order of one frame, the rejection of an invalid selected value, the rounding rule, the single fuel alert, the initial selected values in crew units) are proposed as DD-C8-01 to -05 (`work/C8/issues.md` §4).

### L2-AER-FCP-010

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-010
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall take each crew selection from the crew interface
  defined in ICD-AER-TST, as one time-stamped crew-selection data item.
Rationale:     The crew interface is data, with no model of physical controls (L0-CON-170,
  L1-AER-510). A human through the TST, a test procedure and an RVS agent use the same data item
  (L0-ASM-120, N-320, N-400, SC-230), so a scripted selection at a given time is handled exactly
  as an interactive one. The timestamp is the CV-840 step index with its simulation time; it
  places the selection in a control frame (L2-AER-FCP-080). The panel is the only crew input
  (L1-AER-520, D55): no other path takes a crew selection into the Aeronef.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): this record takes each crew selection as a time-stamped data item of
  the crew interface; L2-AER-FCP-020 closes the list of selections; -080 and -090 take them at the
  next control frame, in a defined order, and give them to autoflight; -100 to -150 check and hold
  the selected values. Together they accept each crew selection of a mode or a target as
  L1-AER-480 requires, and a scripted selection behaves as an interactive one because both arrive
  as this same item.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480
Source:        N-300, N-320, N-360, N-400; L0-ASM-120; CONOPS §4.3, SC-230
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-010; success criteria: a run driven by a selection script, and the same
  run with the same selections entered through the TST interactive interface at the same
  simulation times, give identical crew-selection records in the operational data (timestamp,
  item, selected value, acceptance, target) and the same verdict on each success criterion of
  SC-050 (D59).
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       ICD-AER-TST not yet written (TT-680), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-020

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-020
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall accept as crew selections only a mode selection of
  Stationary, Heading, Altitude hold, Go to altitude, Vertical speed, Go to hover, Takeoff or Land,
  and a selected value of the heading, horizontal speed, altitude or vertical speed selector.
Rationale:     The panel content set by the owner (D71, answers #79): a button that engages each
  mode, Takeoff and Land included (D69), and the readouts of the values the crew sets, horizontal
  speed, vertical speed, target altitude and the heading for Heading (D56). CONOPS §4.3 (CR-B3-32,
  D73) lists eight engage commands; Hover has none, because Go to hover and Takeoff arm it and it
  engages when the vehicle is stationary at the hover height (D56, D69; C8-Q04). There is no
  autoflight disengage selection and no manual control (L0-CON-180, D49). The altitude selector
  is the target altitude selector of D56; its selected value is the selected altitude. The
  provisional engage labels of CONOPS §4.3 (STAT, HDG, ALT, GO ALT, V/S, GO HOVER, TAKEOFF, LAND)
  name the items in ICD-AER-TST. The list is closed: it is the input side of the panel content
  (#79). A data item that names no listed selection is refused by the crew interface (IN-C8-31).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010; this record fixes which selections exist.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480
Source:        D56, D69, D71; #79; CONOPS §4.3, §4.4
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-020; success criteria: ICD-AER-TST defines exactly twelve
  crew-selection items, the eight mode selections and the four selected values listed, and the
  FCP implementation defines no other crew-selection item.
```

### L2-AER-FCP-030

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-030
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall output its annunciations, selector readouts and
  indications at each output sample as the crew-interface data items defined in ICD-AER-TST.
Rationale:     The crew receives annunciations and indications as data (L0-CON-170). The output
  sample period is [P-TM-010] (L1-AER-600), the same clock as the operational data, so what the
  crew sees and what the operational data record line up in time (N-390). Each data item carries
  its crew-facing unit in its ICD definition (CV-760 group 2) and, for an indication, its
  validity (L2-AER-FCP-370). The crew interface output goes to the TST, which shows it to a human
  or hands it to a test procedure (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS I-42); how a human sees it is the TST's,
  not a display format of the Aeronef (L1-AER-510).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): this record outputs the annunciations and indications as ICD-AER-TST
  data items at each output sample; -040 closes the list; -300 forms each sample from the latest
  control frame; -320, -350 and -360 to -380 give each item its content, and -370 its validity.
  Together they are the whole of L1-AER-490.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-490
Source:        N-060, N-340, N-380; CONOPS §4.3
```

### L2-AER-FCP-040

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-040
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall give in its crew interface output only the mode state,
  target, landing gear position and fuel alert annunciations, the readout of each selector and the
  altitude, vertical speed, horizontal speed, heading, track and remaining fuel indications.
Rationale:     The output side of the panel content (#79, D71). The annunciations show the autoflight
  state and the alerts: the armed and active modes and whether autoflight is engaged (the mode
  state), the targets in use, the gear position (L1-AER-470 v2) and the fuel alert (L1-AER-570,
  -580). The selector readouts are the values the crew has set (D71); they are annunciations of
  the selected targets (CONOPS §4.3; CR-C8-02). The indications are the closed list of the
  glossary (*Indication*; CONOPS §4.3; L1-AER-490). There is no flight path angle and no VOR
  quantity (D56); the height above the ground is a guidance quantity of autoflight, not an
  indication. The list is closed so that the panel content is fixed in one place.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030; this record fixes which items exist.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310; this record makes the mode state, the targets and
  the gear position items of the output.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-490, L1-AER-470
Source:        D55, D56, D69, D71; #79; CONOPS §4.3; N-060, N-150
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-040; success criteria: ICD-AER-TST defines the crew interface
  output as exactly these items (four annunciation items, four selector readouts, six
  indications), each with its unit, and the FCP implementation outputs no other crew-interface
  item.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       ICD-AER-TST not yet written (TT-680), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-050

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-050
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall give autoflight, through ICD-FCP, only the mode
  selections it takes, the target of each selector in SI and the selectors whose selected value it
  accepts at that control frame.
Rationale:     The panel → autoflight half of the panel boundary (L1-AER-880, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS
  I-19). Only SI values cross: the crew-facing units stay in the panel (CV-760). AF receives the
  current target of each of the four selectors at each control frame, so it always has the value
  to fly, and it learns which selectors the crew set in this frame, which it needs to tell a new
  selection from an unchanged one (for example a new selected vertical speed during Go to
  altitude, N-090, SC-020). The mode selections are given in order (L2-AER-FCP-090), with no
  judgement of the mode state: arming, activation and refusal are AF's (C5). The selected heading
  arrives as a direction angle in [0, 2π) rad, the selected horizontal speed as a horizontal speed
  in m/s, the selected altitude as an altitude in m and the selected vertical speed as a signed
  vertical speed in m/s (CV-430, CV-480, CV-490, CV-530).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-880): this record is the panel's output across ICD-FCP and -060 its inputs;
  each is closed, so the panel exchanges with its neighbours only the listed kinds of L1-AER-880.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-880
Source:        D55; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §3.2 I-19, §5.1
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-050; success criteria: ICD-FCP defines the panel → autoflight
  data as the ordered mode selections of a control frame, the SI target of each of the four
  selectors and the set of selectors accepted in the frame, each with its SI unit, and the FCP
  implementation gives autoflight nothing else.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       ICD-FCP not yet written (TT-680), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-060

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-060
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall take, through ICD-FCP, only the annunciation content
  from autoflight, the navigation quantities with their validity from navigation and the fuel state
  from the FU subsystem.
Rationale:     The inputs of the panel boundary (L1-AER-880; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS I-06, I-16, I-18).
  Autoflight gives the annunciation content: the mode state, the targets in use and the landing
  gear position, which AF takes from the gear state (L1-AER-470 v2). Navigation gives the
  altitude, vertical speed, horizontal speed, heading and track with their validity, the same
  values it gives autoflight (L1-AER-890), so the crew and the modes see one definition of each
  quantity (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §5.2). The FU subsystem gives the fuel state: the remaining fuel
  mass, the low-fuel indication and the fuel-exhaustion indication (L2-AER-FU-240). Each input is
  in SI. The panel reads each input once per control frame, at the frame start.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-880): see L2-AER-FCP-050.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-880
Source:        D55; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §3.2 I-06, I-16, I-18, §5.1
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-060; success criteria: ICD-FCP defines the inputs of the panel as
  the annunciation content (mode state, targets, gear position), the five indicated navigation
  quantities with their validity and the fuel state (by reference to its vehicle-model boundary
  definition), each in SI, and the FCP implementation reads no other input during a run.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       ICD-FCP not yet written (TT-680), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-070

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-070
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall exchange with the vehicle model only the fuel state,
  taken as the vehicle-model boundary ICD defines it.
Rationale:     The vehicle-model boundary carries effector commands and the landing gear command in,
  and vehicle state, gear state, mass properties and fuel state out (L1-AER-430). Of these the
  panel uses the fuel state only, which ICD-FCP takes by reference to its boundary definition
  (L1-AER-880 rationale, L2-AER-FU-240). The panel gives the vehicle model nothing. The gear
  position reaches the panel through autoflight, as annunciation content, not from the gear
  state directly (L1-AER-470 v2), and the indicated quantities come from navigation, not from the
  vehicle state. Keeping the panel off the rest of the boundary keeps it replaceable with the
  flight software (L0-CON-210).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-430): the fuel state is one of the listed kinds and is defined in the
  boundary ICD; no other panel data item crosses the boundary, which is the FCP share of
  L1-AER-430.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-430
Source:        D55; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §5.1
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-070; success criteria: the only FCP data item that crosses the
  vehicle-model boundary is the fuel state, read by its boundary ICD definition; no FCP
  requirement or implementation item reads the vehicle state, the gear state or the mass
  properties, or writes to the vehicle model.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Vehicle-model boundary ICD home (IN-B1-01, TT-720), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-080

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-080
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     At each control frame, the flight control panel shall take each crew selection whose
  timestamp is later than the start of the previous control frame and at or before the start of
  this control frame, and give it to autoflight in this control frame.
Rationale:     The flight software runs once per control frame, in the order FCP → NAV → AF → FC
  (DD-010, L1-AER-680); the panel takes the selections at step 2 of the frame (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS
  §4.2), so a selection reaches autoflight in the frame that takes it. A selection timestamped
  inside a frame waits at most one control frame [P-SYS-310], which is in the annunciation budget
  (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.5). The first control frame starts at step zero and has no previous
  frame, so it takes each selection timestamped at step zero. Whether an input timestamped k first
  affects step k or step k + 1 is fixed in ICD-AER-TST (CV-840); this record follows the frame
  sequence of FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2. Mode selections are given whatever the mode state; whether
  a mode arms, becomes active or is refused is the mode logic of AF (C5). A selection made on the
  ground or during a Takeoff or a Land is taken the same way.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-680): the panel's crew-selection function executes at each control frame and
  at no other time; with DD-010 it runs in the frame, before autoflight, which is the FCP share
  of L1-AER-680.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-680, L1-AER-480
Source:        FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2; CV-830, CV-840; DD-010
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-080; success criteria: with selections scripted at a frame-start step,
  at a step inside a control frame and at the step that follows a frame start, the ICD-FCP data
  of the control frames (IN-C8-40) show each selection given to autoflight at the first control
  frame whose start is at or later than its timestamp, and at no other frame; a mode selection
  that autoflight refuses (Land with Hover not active) is still given to autoflight.
Parameters:    P-SYS-310
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-310 value TBR (TT-070), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-090

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-090
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the flight control panel takes more than one crew selection at a control frame,
  the flight control panel shall give autoflight the mode selections in the order of their
  timestamps, and of their receipt for equal timestamps, with the selector targets that result
  from each selected value it accepts at that control frame.
Rationale:     Several selections can fall in one frame: a script sets a target and engages a mode
  at the same time (SC-060 step 1, SC-110 leg 2), or a human makes two quick selections. The
  order must be defined so that a repeated run gives the same result (L1-AER-690). The mode
  selections keep their order, so that two mode selections in one frame reach the mode logic as
  the crew made them. The selector targets given with them are those after each selected value of
  the frame has been checked and applied, so that a mode engaged in the same frame as its target
  uses the new target, whatever the order of the two in the frame. A frame lasts [P-SYS-310], so
  the crew cannot tell the two orders apart. Two selected values of one selector in one frame:
  the last one accepted holds. This is design decision DD-C8-01 (proposed), inside this record.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): with -150 and -540, this record makes the panel output a function of
  the selections and their order only, which is the FCP share of L1-AER-690.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-690, L1-AER-480
Source:        N-330; CONOPS SC-060, SC-110; DD-C8-01 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-090; success criteria: with two mode selections, and with a selected
  value and a mode selection, scripted in one control frame in each order and with equal
  timestamps, the ICD-FCP data of that frame (IN-C8-40) give the mode selections in script order
  and the targets of the selected values of the frame; with two selected values of one selector
  in one frame, the target is that of the later one.
Parameters:    P-SYS-310
TBD/TBR:       P-SYS-310 value TBR (TT-070), Martin, G2 (I1); DD-C8-01 owner acceptance, Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-100

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-100
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall accept a selected heading, in degrees, that is a
  multiple of [P-AF-040] within the direction-angle range of CV-520.
Rationale:     The heading selector of Heading mode (D56, D71). The selected heading is a heading,
  the direction in which the nose points (CV-530), not a track. The direction-angle range of
  CV-520 is from zero, north, up to but not including a full turn, so 360° is not a selectable
  value and north is selected as zero (C8-Q02); every heading has exactly one selected value. The
  selection resolution [P-AF-040] is 1° (D71, TBR). A value the selector does not accept is
  handled by L2-AER-FCP-140. The unit is the CV-740 crew-facing unit for heading (CV-760 group 1).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010. Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480, L1-AER-500
Source:        D56, D71; CONOPS §4.3, SC-060, SC-120; CV-520, CV-530, CV-740
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-100; success criteria: selected headings of zero, of [P-AF-040], of 180°
  and of the largest multiple of [P-AF-040] below a full turn are accepted (the selector readout
  shows them); 360°, a negative value and a value that is not a multiple of [P-AF-040] are not
  accepted (the readout keeps the previous value).
Parameters:    P-AF-040
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-040 value TBR (TT-170), Martin, G2 (C8); north selected as zero (C8-Q02),
  Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-110

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-110
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall accept a selected horizontal speed, in knots, that is
  a non-negative multiple of [P-AF-030] whose exact CV-750 conversion is at most [P-SYS-230].
Rationale:     The horizontal speed selector of Heading mode, from zero to the vehicle's maximum
  (D56). Zero is selectable: with no horizontal speed, a heading change turns the vehicle in
  place (SC-120). The selected horizontal speed is a ground speed: its target is a horizontal
  speed (CV-480), over the ground, and [P-SYS-230] is a ground speed (D72 Q1); with wind the
  airspeed differs. [P-SYS-230] is the top of the selectable horizontal speed (L1-AER-310
  rationale). The range check uses the exact conversion of the selected value (in rational
  arithmetic, or against the limit converted to crew units), not its floating-point target, so a
  limit that is an exact crew value is selectable whatever the order of the operations (the same
  rule in -120 and -130). [P-SYS-230] is 300 kt (D66); for 300 kt to be accepted, the database
  value of [P-SYS-230] must be at least the exact conversion of 300 kt, which its present
  three-decimal value (299.9994 kt) is not (C8-Q07, `work/C8/param_proposals.md`). The resolution
  [P-AF-030] is 1 kt (D71, TBR).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-310): the vehicle can sustain each horizontal speed up to [P-SYS-230] under
  autoflight only if the crew can select it; this record and -120 (the altitude range of the
  operating volume) give the panel side, and AF, FC and EF fly it.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010. Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480, L1-AER-500, L1-AER-310
Source:        D56, D66, D71, D72; CONOPS §4.3, SC-060, SC-120; CV-480
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-110; success criteria: selected horizontal speeds of zero, of [P-AF-030]
  and of the largest multiple of [P-AF-030] whose exact CV-750 conversion is at most [P-SYS-230]
  (300 kt with the proposed database value) are accepted; the next multiple, a negative value and a value
  that is not a multiple of [P-AF-030] are not accepted.
Parameters:    P-AF-030, P-SYS-230
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-030 value TBR (TT-170), Martin, G2 (C8); P-SYS-230 stored precision (C8-Q07),
  Martin, G2 (I1); FCP allocation of L1-AER-310 (CR-C8-01), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-120

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-120
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall accept a selected altitude, in feet, that is a
  multiple of [P-AF-010] whose exact CV-750 conversion is at least [P-SYS-210] plus half of
  [P-DYN-030] and at most [P-SYS-200].
Rationale:     The target altitude selector of Go to altitude and Vertical speed (D56). The target
  is an altitude, of the centre of gravity (CV-430). The lower bound is the altitude at which the
  height above the ground is the minimum operating height [P-SYS-210] at level attitude (CV-400;
  L1-AER-330 v2; the P-SYS-210 description); pitch and roll are held at zero (D57), so the level
  geometry applies. With the present values it is 3.524 m (11.56 ft), so the lowest selectable
  value is 20 ft. That answers the B3 impact-list question for C8: the bound needs no rounding and
  no parameter of its own, because the rule picks the lowest multiple of [P-AF-010] at or above it,
  and it follows a change of [P-SYS-210] or [P-DYN-030]. The upper bound is the maximum operating
  altitude [P-SYS-200] (150 000 ft, a multiple of [P-AF-010]). The check uses the exact
  conversion, as in -110. The resolution [P-AF-010] is 10 ft (D71, TBR).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-330): a descent levels off at the selected altitude (D56); this record bounds
  the selectable altitude below at the minimum operating height, as the L1-AER-330 rationale
  assigns to C8.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-310): this record bounds the selectable altitude above at the top of the
  operating volume, so a selected altitude is inside the volume in which L1-AER-310 applies.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010. Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480, L1-AER-500, L1-AER-330, L1-AER-310
Source:        D56, D67, D70, D71; CONOPS §4.2, §4.3, SC-020; CV-400, CV-430; B3 impact list (C8)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-120; success criteria: the lowest multiple of [P-AF-010] whose exact CV-750
  conversion is at least [P-SYS-210] plus half of [P-DYN-030] (20 ft with the present values) and
  the multiple equal to [P-SYS-200] (150 000 ft) are accepted, and a mid value is accepted; the
  multiple below the lower one, the multiple above [P-SYS-200] and a value that is not a multiple
  of [P-AF-010] are not accepted.
Parameters:    P-AF-010, P-SYS-210, P-DYN-030, P-SYS-200
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-010 value TBR (TT-170), Martin, G2 (C8); P-SYS-200, P-SYS-210 values TBR
  (TT-010), Martin, G2 (I1); P-DYN-030 value TBR (TT-740), Martin, 2026-11-30; FCP allocation of
  L1-AER-310, -330 (CR-C8-01), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-130

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-130
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall accept a selected vertical speed, in feet per minute
  and positive when climbing, that is a multiple of [P-AF-020] whose exact CV-750 conversion is at
  least minus [P-SYS-250] and at most [P-SYS-240].
Rationale:     The vertical speed selector of Vertical speed mode, and of Go to altitude, where it
  replaces the standard vertical speed [P-AF-220] (D56, D70; CONOPS §4.3). The selected value is
  signed as the vertical speed (CV-490): a climb is positive, a descent negative. [P-SYS-240] is
  the top of the selectable climb vertical speed (L1-AER-320 rationale) and [P-SYS-250] the
  largest selectable descent magnitude, both 6000 ft/min (D67). The check uses the exact
  conversion, as in -110: in floating point, 6000 × 0.3048 / 60 exceeds 30.48 by one rounding
  step, and a limit check on that target would refuse the maximum. Zero is a multiple of [P-AF-020]
  and is selectable; what Vertical speed does with a zero or a wrong-signed selected vertical
  speed is AF's (C5, SC-040 TBR; C8-Q08). The standard vertical speed is not a selected value: AF
  uses it until the crew selects another (D56), and AF knows when that happens from the selectors
  accepted in each frame (L2-AER-FCP-050). The resolution [P-AF-020] is 100 ft/min (D71, TBR).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-320): the vehicle can climb at each vertical speed up to [P-SYS-240] under
  autoflight only if the crew can select it; this record gives the panel side.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-330): likewise for the descent down to minus [P-SYS-250]; with -120 (the
  lowest selectable altitude) it is the FCP share of L1-AER-330.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010. Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480, L1-AER-500, L1-AER-320, L1-AER-330
Source:        D56, D67, D70, D71; CONOPS §4.3, SC-020, SC-040; CV-490
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-130; success criteria: selected vertical speeds of minus the multiple
  of [P-AF-020] equal to [P-SYS-250], of zero, of [P-AF-020] and of the multiple equal to
  [P-SYS-240] are accepted; the multiples beyond each end and a value that is not a multiple of
  [P-AF-020] are not accepted.
Parameters:    P-AF-020, P-SYS-240, P-SYS-250, P-AF-220
TBD/TBR:       P-AF-020 value TBR (TT-170), Martin, G2 (C8); P-SYS-240, P-SYS-250 values TBR
  (TT-010), Martin, G2 (I1); zero selectable (C8-Q08), Martin, G2; FCP allocation of L1-AER-320,
  -330 (CR-C8-01), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-140

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-140
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     If the flight control panel takes a selected value that its selector does not accept,
  then the flight control panel shall keep the previous selected value of that selector.
Rationale:     A selected value outside its selectable range or not a multiple of its selection
  resolution (L2-AER-FCP-100 to -130) is refused, not clamped to the range and not rounded to the
  resolution. Rounding would change the value before conversion, which CV-770 and L1-AER-870
  exclude; clamping would hide an error in a selection script, and the run would no longer be what
  the test procedure asked for (D59; the same choice as DD-140 for the initial fuel). The refusal
  is visible: the selector readout keeps its value, autoflight is not told of a new selection
  (L2-AER-FCP-050), and the operational data record the selection as not accepted
  (L2-AER-FCP-500). This is design decision DD-C8-02 (proposed; C8-Q01).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010; this record defines the outcome of a selection the
  panel does not accept.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480
Source:        CV-770; D59; DD-140; DD-C8-02 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-140; success criteria: for each selector, a selected value above its
  range, below its range and off its resolution, each scripted after an accepted value, leaves the
  selector readout and the target given to autoflight at the accepted value, and the operational
  data record each refused selection as not accepted.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Refusal rather than clamping or rounding (C8-Q01, DD-C8-02), Martin, G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-150

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-150
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall change the selected value of a selector only when it
  accepts a selected value of that selector.
Rationale:     A selected value stays until the crew changes it (N-090: the crew changes a target
  and sees the vehicle go to it). Autoflight has no data path into the selectors: across ICD-FCP
  it gives the panel annunciation content only (L1-AER-880), so no mode change resets or
  synchronises a selector. A target that autoflight sets for itself, for example the altitude
  that Altitude hold holds when engaged directly (SC-050 step 5), the reference position of
  Stationary or the standard vertical speed of Go to altitude, is AF's, and the panel annunciates
  it as a target (L2-AER-FCP-330), not as a selected value. The selected value is held in its
  crew-facing unit, as selected, so its readout and its target never pass through a round trip
  of conversions (L2-AER-FCP-200, -350).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): see L2-AER-FCP-090. Sufficiency (L1-AER-480): see L2-AER-FCP-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-480, L1-AER-690
Source:        N-090; CONOPS §4.4 (targets); D55
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-150; success criteria: in the reference mission (SC-110), the readout
  and the SI target of each selector change only at the control frames that accept a selection of
  that selector, and are unchanged across each mode change, capture, takeoff, landing and the
  fuel exhaustion.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```
