### L2-AER-FCP-200

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-200
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     When the flight control panel accepts a selected value, the flight control panel
  shall set its target to the selected value multiplied by the exact CV-750 factor of its
  crew-facing unit, without rounding.
Rationale:     CV-770: a selected value becomes its SI target by multiplication by the exact factor,
  with no intermediate rounding (L1-AER-870). The factors are 0.3048 m per ft, 0.3048/60 m/s per
  ft/min, 1852/3600 m/s per kt (used as the fraction, CV-750) and π/180 rad per degree. A selected
  heading in the CV-520 range gives a target in [0, 2π) without wrapping, and the sign of a
  selected vertical speed is kept. "Without rounding" excludes any rounding of the value to a
  resolution or to fewer digits; the floating-point representation of the product is the only
  difference allowed, bounded in the test by the relative tolerance [P-FCP-020]. The target is
  computed once, at acceptance, from the selected value held in its crew unit (L2-AER-FCP-150).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-870): this record converts each crew selection to SI by the exact factor
  without rounding; -210 and -220 convert each shown quantity from SI by the same factor with one
  rounding at the end, so together they are the whole of L1-AER-870.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-870
Source:        N-070; D7, D34; CV-750, CV-770
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-200; success criteria: for each selector and for selected values at
  both ends of its range and at three values inside it, the SI target that the panel gives
  autoflight equals the selected value times the CV-750 factor, computed independently, within the
  relative tolerance [P-FCP-020].
Parameters:    P-FCP-020
TBD/TBR:       P-FCP-020 new, value TBR (`work/C8/param_proposals.md`), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-210

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-210
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall compute the shown value of each annunciated target and
  each indication by dividing its SI value by the exact CV-750 factor of its crew-facing unit and
  rounding the result half away from zero to the nearest multiple of its display resolution.
Rationale:     The display resolution and the rounding rule are FCP requirement matters (CV-780).
  The display resolutions are [P-TM-020] for altitude (1 ft), [P-TM-030] for vertical speed
  (10 ft/min), [P-TM-040] for horizontal speed (1 kt), [P-TM-050] for heading and track (1°), all
  D71, TBR, and [P-FCP-010] for the remaining fuel (1 lb, proposed). The conversion uses the
  inverse of the CV-750 factor with no intermediate rounding (CV-750, L1-AER-870), and then one
  rounding, half away from zero, which is symmetric for a signed vertical speed. A target that the
  crew selected converts back to its selected value up to floating-point error, which the rounding
  removes, because each selection resolution is a multiple of the display resolution of its
  quantity. The selector readouts are not converted (L2-AER-FCP-350). A remaining fuel mass below
  half of [P-FCP-010] shows as zero before fuel exhaustion; the fuel alert, not the shown value,
  marks the exhaustion (L2-AER-FCP-400). This is design decision DD-C8-03 (proposed; C8-Q06).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-870): see L2-AER-FCP-200.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230; this record gives each shown value in its CV-740
  unit.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-870, L1-AER-500
Source:        N-070; D7, D9, D71; CV-750, CV-780; #55; DD-C8-03 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-210; success criteria: with a test source at ICD-FCP (IN-C8-40), for SI
  values of each quantity at a multiple of its display resolution, just below and just above half
  a resolution step from it, and at negative vertical speeds, the shown value equals the
  independently computed quotient rounded half away from zero to the display resolution.
Parameters:    P-TM-020, P-TM-030, P-TM-040, P-TM-050, P-FCP-010
TBD/TBR:       P-TM-020 to P-TM-050 values TBR (TT-210), Martin, G2 (C8); P-FCP-010 new, value TBR
  (C8-Q06), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-220

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-220
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall show each heading and each track in the
  direction-angle range of CV-520, with a value that rounds to a full turn shown as zero.
Rationale:     A heading or track just below a full turn (for example 359.6°) rounds to a full turn
  under L2-AER-FCP-210; north is shown as zero, so the shown value stays in the CV-520 range,
  matches the selectable heading range (L2-AER-FCP-100) and each direction has one shown value.
  CV-520 leaves the crew-facing value of north to a display requirement; this is that requirement
  (C8-Q02). How the number is laid out ("0" or "000") is a display format, outside the Aeronef
  (L1-AER-510). Annunciated heading targets follow the same rule.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-870): see L2-AER-FCP-200.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-870, L1-AER-500
Source:        CV-520, CV-920; DD-C8-03 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-220; success criteria: with a test source at ICD-FCP (IN-C8-40),
  headings and tracks of zero, of just below a full turn by less than half of [P-TM-050], of just
  below by more than half of [P-TM-050] and of half a turn are shown as zero, zero, the largest
  multiple of [P-TM-050] below a full turn and 180°.
Parameters:    P-TM-050
TBD/TBR:       P-TM-050 value TBR (TT-210), Martin, G2 (C8); north shown as zero (C8-Q02), Martin,
  G2
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-230

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-230
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The flight control panel shall state each selected value, selector readout,
  annunciated target and indication in the crew-facing unit that CV-740 assigns to its quantity.
Rationale:     North American aviation units for everything the crew selects or sees (D7, D9, D34;
  L0-CON-270): altitude in ft, vertical speed in ft/min, horizontal speed in kt, heading and track
  in degrees, fuel in lb. A selected value and its readout are in the unit of the quantity of
  their selector; an annunciated target is in the unit of the quantity it applies to. CV-760 lets
  crew-facing units appear only in crew-selection and crew-presentation requirements (groups 1
  and 2): these FCP records are those, and every other FCP quantity, at ICD-FCP and in the
  vehicle-model boundary, is in SI (L2-AER-FCP-050, -060). The selection and display resolution
  parameters are stored in their crew units (CV-790).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): this record names the CV-740 unit of every crew-facing item;
  L2-AER-FCP-100 to -130 name it for each selector, -210 and -220 compute each shown value in it,
  -350 shows each readout in it and -380 the fuel in pounds. Together each crew selection and
  each annunciated or indicated quantity is in its CV-740 unit.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-500
Source:        N-070; D7, D9, D34; CV-740, CV-760, CV-790
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Inspection — IN-FCP-230; success criteria: each crew-selection item and each
  crew-interface output item in ICD-AER-TST, and each FCP requirement on a crew selection or a
  crew-facing output, names the CV-740 unit of its quantity; no FCP item at ICD-FCP or at the
  vehicle-model boundary is in a crew-facing unit.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       ICD-AER-TST and ICD-FCP not yet written (TT-680), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-300

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-300
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     At each output sample, the flight control panel shall form its crew interface output
  from the inputs it took and the selected values it held at the latest control frame that starts
  at or before that output sample.
Rationale:     The panel reads its inputs once per control frame (L2-AER-FCP-060) and outputs at
  each output sample [P-TM-010] (L1-AER-490). This record ties the two: a sample shows the
  frame's data, never a mix of two frames, so the annunciations, readouts and indications of a
  sample agree with each other and with the operational data of that sample. [P-TM-010] is
  proposed equal to [P-SYS-310] (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.5), so each frame is shown once; if it is
  a multiple, the samples skip frames, and the latency stays within [P-TM-010] (L2-AER-FCP-310).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-490, L1-AER-470
Source:        FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §4.2, §7.5; CV-830
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-300; success criteria: in the reference mission (SC-110), each output
  sample's crew interface output equals the output computed independently from the ICD-FCP inputs
  and the selected values of the latest control frame that starts at or before the sample
  (IN-C8-40).
Parameters:    P-TM-010, P-SYS-310
TBD/TBR:       P-TM-010, P-SYS-310 values TBR (TT-070), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-310

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-310
Level:         L2
Type:          Performance
Statement:     When an input of the flight control panel or a selected value it holds changes at a
  control frame, the flight control panel shall present the change in its crew interface output
  within [P-TM-010] of the start of that control frame.
Rationale:     The FCP share of the annunciation latency [P-SYS-470] is one output sample period
  [P-TM-010] (L1-AER-470; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.5). The rest is upstream: AF decides the content
  within its share [P-AF-060] (C5), and a crew selection waits at most one control frame
  [P-SYS-310] before the panel takes it (L2-AER-FCP-080). For the fuel alerts, the FU subsystem
  sets the indication in the step at which the condition first holds (its share is zero,
  L2-AER-FU-220, -230) and the panel reads it at the next frame start, at most one control frame
  later, so the alert is shown within [P-SYS-310] plus [P-TM-010] of the condition, well inside
  [P-SYS-470] (0.04 s against 0.5 s with the proposed values). Measured as CV-860, from the start
  of the frame at which the input or selected value changes to the first output sample that shows
  it.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): a change of mode state, target or gear position given by AF, or of a
  selected value, reaches the output within [P-TM-010] of its frame (this record); -320, -330,
  -340 and -350 make each of them an annunciation, and -040 and -300 place them in each sample.
  With AF's share [P-AF-060] and the frame wait of -080, the change is annunciated within
  [P-SYS-470].
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-570, L1-AER-580): with -390 and -400 (the alert content) and FU's zero share,
  this record keeps each fuel alert within [P-SYS-470] of its condition.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-470, L1-AER-570, L1-AER-580
Source:        N-060, N-150, N-210; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §7.5; CONOPS §4.4, §4.5
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-310; success criteria: in the mode-transition tests and the reference
  mission (SC-110), for each change of the annunciation content, of a navigation quantity, of the
  fuel state and of a selected value, the first output sample showing it is within [P-TM-010] of
  the start of the control frame at which the panel took it; each LOW FUEL and FUEL EXHAUSTED
  annunciation is within [P-SYS-310] plus [P-TM-010] of the first simulation step at which its
  fuel-state condition holds.
Parameters:    P-TM-010, P-SYS-310, P-SYS-470, P-AF-060
TBD/TBR:       P-TM-010, P-SYS-310, P-SYS-470 values TBR (TT-070), P-AF-060 value TBD (TT-070),
  Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-320

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-320
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall annunciate each item of the mode state in the
  annunciation content with the label that AF_MODE_LOGIC assigns to that item.
Rationale:     The crew needs to know which mode is active, which is armed and what the vehicle
  will do next, from the annunciations alone (N-060, N-210). The mode state is the active
  horizontal and vertical modes (or the one mode that governs both axes), the armed modes and
  whether autoflight is engaged (glossary *Mode state*). AF decides the content and the labels
  (L1-AER-470: the labels are C5's; CONOPS §4.4 gives provisional ones), so the panel shows the
  label that AF_MODE_LOGIC gives each mode in each state and adds none of its own; the mode logic
  and its annunciation are then defined in one place. With no mode active, for example on the
  ground before a Takeoff, each item is annunciated as empty. What the mode state is after fuel
  exhaustion is AF's (D68, TT-580); the panel shows it the same way.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-470, L1-AER-490
Source:        N-060, N-210; CONOPS §4.4; D56, D68
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-320; success criteria: in the mode-transition tests and the reference
  mission (SC-110), at each output sample, the mode-state annunciation holds, for each item of the
  annunciation content of the latest control frame, the AF_MODE_LOGIC label of that item, and no
  other mode label.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       AF_MODE_LOGIC not yet written (TT-670), Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-330

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-330
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall annunciate each target of the annunciation content as
  its shown value, with the quantity to which the target applies.
Rationale:     The crew needs to see the targets the active and armed modes are flying (N-060): for
  example the altitude that Altitude hold is armed to capture, the vertical speed Go to altitude
  is using (the standard one or the selected one), the altitude Altitude hold holds when engaged
  directly, or the hover height. AF decides which targets are in the content and gives them in SI
  (C5); the panel converts each with L2-AER-FCP-210 and -220 and names its quantity (altitude,
  vertical speed, horizontal speed or heading). A target that came from a selected value shows the
  selected value, because each selection resolution is a multiple of the display resolution
  (L2-AER-FCP-210). The selector readouts are a separate item (L2-AER-FCP-350): a readout is what
  the crew set, a target annunciation is what the modes use.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-470
Source:        N-060; CONOPS §4.3, §4.4, SC-020, SC-050
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-330; success criteria: in SC-020, SC-050 and the reference mission
  (SC-110), at each output sample, the target annunciation holds each target of the annunciation
  content of the latest control frame, with its quantity, as its shown value per -210 and -220.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Annunciated targets per mode (AF_MODE_LOGIC, TT-670), Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-340

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-340
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall annunciate the landing gear position of the
  annunciation content as GEAR DOWN when the gear is extended, GEAR UP when it is retracted and
  GEAR TRANSIT when it is in transit.
Rationale:     The gear position is annunciated because a landing needs the gear extended and the
  crew needs to know what the vehicle will do next (L1-AER-470 v2, N-060). AF passes it from the
  gear state as annunciation content (L1-AER-880). The gear position is not a mode, so its labels
  are the panel's, like the fuel alerts; CONOPS SC-130 left them to C5 and C8, and C8 proposes
  these (C8-Q03). The three labels cover the three gear positions (glossary *Gear position*). No
  crew-facing unit applies.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310; this record makes the gear position an
  annunciation.

  gear position in the annunciation content of the latest control frame is extended, retracted or
```

### L2-AER-FCP-350

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-350
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall give the readout of each selector as the selected
  value that the selector holds, in the crew-facing unit in which it was selected, with no
  conversion.
Rationale:     The panel shows the values the crew sets: horizontal speed in kt, vertical speed in
  ft/min, target altitude in ft and the selected heading in degrees (D71). The readout is the
  selected value itself, as held (L2-AER-FCP-150), so the crew reads back exactly what it set,
  with no conversion round trip and no display rounding; each selected value is already a
  multiple of its selection resolution. A refused selection leaves the readout unchanged
  (L2-AER-FCP-140). Each readout is present at each output sample, whatever the mode state: the
  heading readout is the selector of Heading mode, shown in each mode.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-470): see L2-AER-FCP-310; a change of a selected target is annunciated in
  its readout. Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-500): see L2-AER-FCP-230.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-470, L1-AER-490, L1-AER-500
Source:        D71; CONOPS §4.3; N-060, N-070
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-350; success criteria: in SC-050 and the reference mission (SC-110), at
  each output sample, each selector readout equals the last accepted selected value of that
  selector (or its initial selected value), in its CV-740 unit, digit for digit.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-360

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-360
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The flight control panel shall indicate the altitude, vertical speed, horizontal
  speed, heading and track of the navigation quantities as their shown values.
Rationale:     The crew sees the vehicle quantities (L1-AER-490 rationale; glossary *Indication*;
  CONOPS §4.3): altitude in ft, vertical speed in ft/min, horizontal speed in kt, heading and track
  in degrees. Navigation computes them once, for autoflight and the panel alike (L1-AER-530,
  -890), and the panel only converts and rounds them (L2-AER-FCP-210, -220). The horizontal speed
  is the ground speed (CV-480). Heading and track are shown separately because they are
  independent on the Aeronef (CV-550). On the ground at rest, the altitude indication is the
  altitude of the centre of gravity, [P-DYN-030]/2 plus [P-DYN-040] (11 ft with the present
  values), not zero, because altitude is measured at the centre of gravity (CV-430).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-490
Source:        N-060, N-380; CONOPS §4.3; CV-430 to CV-550
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-360; success criteria: in the reference mission (SC-110), at each output
  sample, each of the five indications equals the shown value computed independently from the
  navigation quantity of the latest control frame (IN-C8-40), including the ground rests.
Parameters:    P-DYN-030, P-DYN-040
TBD/TBR:       P-DYN-030 value TBR (TT-740), Martin, 2026-11-30; P-DYN-040 value TBR (TT-830),
  Martin, G2 (C1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-370

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-370
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While a navigation quantity is not valid, the flight control panel shall give its
  indication as not valid, with no shown value.
Rationale:     Track is undefined at zero horizontal speed, and heading near ±90° of pitch (CV-540,
  CV-580). Navigation gives the validity with each quantity, from the thresholds [P-NAV-010] and
  [P-NAV-030] (L1-AER-890, C6). A value shown while its quantity is undefined would mislead the
  crew; the panel shows that the indication is not valid instead. Track is not valid on the
  ground, in Hover, in Stationary and in a turn in place, which are ordinary states. Heading is
  not expected to be invalid, because pitch is held at zero (D57), but the rule covers it. The
  panel uses the validity that navigation gives and sets no threshold of its own, so the crew and
  autoflight see the same validity (CV-580 leaves the behaviour to NAV, AF and FCP, CR-B1-13).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-490): see L2-AER-FCP-030; this record gives the indications their validity.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-490
Source:        CV-540, CV-580; CR-B1-13; CONOPS §4.2
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-370; success criteria: in SC-040 and SC-120 (zero horizontal speed),
  in SC-130 on the ground and in a test source run at ICD-FCP (IN-C8-40) with each quantity
  flagged not valid, the indication of each quantity flagged not valid is given as not valid
  with no shown value at each output sample, and as a shown value at each sample at which it is
  valid.
Parameters:    P-NAV-010, P-NAV-030
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-010, P-NAV-030 values TBD (TT-200), Martin, G2 (C6)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-380
```

### L2-AER-FCP-390

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-390
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the fuel state indicates low fuel and does not indicate fuel exhaustion, the
  flight control panel shall annunciate LOW FUEL as the fuel alert.
Rationale:     The crew needs a warning before the fuel runs out (N-150); running out is the crew's
  to avoid, with this alert (D68). The low-fuel indication is set by FU when the remaining fuel
  mass is below the fraction [P-FU-020] of the capacity [P-FU-010] (L2-AER-FU-220), so a run that
  starts below it shows LOW FUEL from its first sample, on the ground included. The label is the
  CONOPS one (§4.5, SC-110); the alert label is L2 and the panel's (L1-AER-570, IN-C2-43). The
  fuel alert is one annunciation item with one label at a time: at fuel exhaustion FUEL EXHAUSTED
  replaces LOW FUEL (L2-AER-FCP-400), as C2 suggested. This is design decision DD-C8-04 (proposed).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-570): FU sets the low-fuel indication at the step at which the fuel falls
  below the threshold; this record annunciates LOW FUEL while it is set; -410 clears the alert
  while it is not; -310 bounds the delay so that the alert comes within [P-SYS-470].
Parent(s):     L1-AER-570
Source:        N-150; CONOPS §4.5, SC-110; D68; IN-C2-43; DD-C8-04 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-390; success criteria: in the reference mission (SC-110), the fuel alert
  is LOW FUEL at each output sample whose latest control frame read a fuel state with the low-fuel
  indication set and the fuel-exhaustion indication clear, and is not LOW FUEL at any other
  sample; in a run started on the ground below the threshold, it is LOW FUEL at the first sample.
Parameters:    P-FU-020, P-FU-010
TBD/TBR:       P-FU-020 value TBR (TT-130), P-FU-010 value TBR (TT-120), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-FCP-400

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-400
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the fuel state indicates fuel exhaustion, the flight control panel shall
  annunciate FUEL EXHAUSTED as the fuel alert.
Rationale:     The crew needs to know when the fuel has run out and what follows (N-150): the
  effectors stop, and the ground contact that follows in flight is a crash (D68, L1-AER-200). The
  fuel-exhaustion indication is set by FU from the first step with zero fuel to the end of the run
  (L2-AER-FU-230), so the alert holds to the end of the run; in a run that starts on the ground
  with no fuel it holds from the first sample (SC-130 zero-fuel variation). The label is the
  CONOPS one (§4.5, SC-110). It replaces LOW FUEL in the one fuel-alert item (DD-C8-04, proposed),
  because the more severe state is the one the crew must see; the low-fuel indication stays set
  in the fuel state (L2-AER-FU-220). What the mode annunciations show after exhaustion is AF's
  (D68, TT-580).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-580): FU sets the exhaustion indication at the first step with zero fuel;
  this record annunciates FUEL EXHAUSTED while it is set; -310 bounds the delay so that it comes
  within [P-SYS-470].
Parent(s):     L1-AER-580
Source:        N-150; CONOPS §4.5, SC-110, SC-130; D68; IN-C2-43; DD-C8-04 (proposed)
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-FCP-400; success criteria: in the reference mission (SC-110) and in a run
  started on the ground with zero fuel, the fuel alert is FUEL EXHAUSTED at each output sample
  whose latest control frame read a fuel state with the fuel-exhaustion indication set, and never
  LOW FUEL at those samples.
Parameters:    —

Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
```

### L2-AER-FCP-410

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-410
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the fuel state indicates neither low fuel nor fuel exhaustion, the flight
  control panel shall give the fuel alert with no label.
Rationale:     An alert that stayed on when its condition does not hold would make the alert
  meaningless (N-150). FU never refuels the vehicle during a run (L2-AER-FU-100), so in practice
  the alert clears only at the start of a run with fuel at or above the threshold; the record
  still closes the decision table of the fuel alert: no label, LOW FUEL (-390) or FUEL EXHAUSTED
  (-400), one at a time (DD-C8-04, proposed).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-570, L1-AER-580): with -390 and -400 this record makes the fuel alert follow
  the fuel state in each of its three cases, so each alert appears for its condition and for no
  other.
```

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-500
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The flight control panel shall give the operational data output, for each crew
  selection it takes, the selection with its timestamp, its selected value in its crew-facing
  unit, whether the panel accepted it and, for an accepted selected value, its target.
Rationale:     Crew inputs are the first thing the operational data relate in time (L0-OBJ-140,
  L1-AER-610; REVERSE_ENGINEERING §3C). The target is recorded in SI with, where the crew
  selected it, the crew-facing value (CV-760 group 4). A selection the panel refuses
  (L2-AER-FCP-140) is recorded too, as not accepted, so a test can tell a refused selection from
  a lost one. A mode selection is recorded as taken; whether autoflight acted on it is the mode
  state that AF gives to the operational data (L1-AER-620). The timestamp is the one the crew
  interface gave (CV-840); TM records the selection with the frame at which the panel took it.
  Whether each signal is releasable is decided with the RVS (#69).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-610): this record gives TM each crew selection with its timestamp, its
  value and the target that results from it, which is the FCP share; TM records it and AF gives
  the mode state that results.
```

```req
Level:         L2
```

```req
  panel holds (L1-AER-660 v2, FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS I-28). They are given in crew-facing units, as

  readout starts exact (C8-Q05, DD-C8-05 proposed). The four initial selected values are the FCP
  item of the initial condition; the mode state and the targets that autoflight sets for itself

  a ground start no mode is active (recommended to C5, #60), and the selectors simply hold the
  values for the first selections after the Takeoff. At the first control frame the panel gives
  autoflight the SI targets of these values (L2-AER-FCP-050), with no selection accepted.
```

### L2-AER-FCP-530

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-530
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     If an initial selected value is one that its selector does not accept, then the
  flight control panel shall give the TST an initial-condition error that names the selector and
  the value.

  (L2-AER-FCP-100 to -130) is rejected, not clamped and not rounded, as an initial fuel mass is
  (DD-140): the run stays what the test procedure asked for (D59). The initial-condition error is
  the data item by which the Aeronef tells the TST that an item of the initial condition is out of
  range; the run does not start (glossary). There is no previous value to keep at the start, so
  the refusal rule of L2-AER-FCP-140 cannot apply. Part of DD-C8-05 (proposed).
```

### L2-AER-FCP-540

```req
ID:            L2-AER-FCP-540
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The flight control panel shall compute each of its outputs only from the initial
  selected values, the crew selections and the ICD-FCP inputs of the run.
Rationale:     Repeat runs must give the same pass or fail verdicts (L1-AER-690, D59). The panel
  adds no variation if its outputs depend on nothing but the run's own inputs: no wall-clock time,
  no random source, no state carried over from another run or simulation instance, and no
  dependence on how fast the run executes (CV-810). The order of the selections within a frame is
  fixed by L2-AER-FCP-090. Bit-identical results are not required (D25, D59); the tolerances are
  those of each success criterion (D63 Q4).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): see L2-AER-FCP-090.
```

<!-- END SECTION FCP -->

<!-- BEGIN SECTION NAV -->
## NAV — Navigation

The navigation requirements (task C6). The subject of each statement is the navigation subsystem (NAV), one of the four flight software subsystems with flight control (FC), autoflight (AF) and the flight control panel (FCP) (glossary *Flight software*, D55). NAV implements function F-AER-09 (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §6.1). It runs once per control frame `[P-SYS-310]`, after the flight control panel and before autoflight (DD-010). Its only input is the **frame-start state**, the vehicle state of the first simulation step of the control frame, which the dynamics model gives it exactly (L1-AER-440, L2-AER-DYN-330). Its outputs are the navigation quantities of the frame, each with its validity, to autoflight (ICD-NAV-AF), to the flight control panel (ICD-FCP) and to the operational data output.

**What NAV computes.** Seven quantities, each by its CONVENTIONS definition and nothing else: the altitude (CV-430), the height above the ground (CV-400), the horizontal position (CV-410), the horizontal speed (CV-480), the vertical speed (CV-490), the heading (CV-530, by CV-260) and the track (CV-540). The first six are the navigation quantities of L1-AER-530 and -890. The horizontal position is added because the modes that hold a position over the ground (Stationary, Hover, Takeoff, Land) need it and autoflight receives no vehicle state (L1-AER-440 gives the state to NAV and FC only); it traces to L1-AER-450, and CR-C6-01 proposes adding it to L1-AER-530, -890 and the glossary (`work/C6/issues.md`). Each quantity is in SI (CV-720, CV-730); the crew-facing units stay in the panel (CV-760, L1-AER-870).

**Validity (CV-580).** The track is valid while the horizontal speed is at least `[P-NAV-010]`, and its value is zero while it is invalid (DD-C6-01). The heading is valid while the magnitude of the pitch angle is at most `[P-NAV-030]`; its value stays the CV-260 yaw angle, which is defined at every attitude. The other five quantities are defined at every state and the state is exact, so they are valid at each frame; they carry a validity all the same, so that each quantity crosses the interfaces in one form and a later sensor model (#83) changes values and flags, not the interfaces. Pitch and roll are held at zero in every mode (D57, L1-AER-390), so the heading is valid in nominal flight; the threshold matters for airborne test starts and after fuel exhaustion, when the vehicle keeps its angular rate and can tumble. On the ground, and in Stationary, Hover, Takeoff and Land, the horizontal speed is below `[P-NAV-010]` and the track is invalid.

**Altitude and height above the ground (TT-820, D69, D70, D73).** The altitude is that of the centre of gravity above the datum plane (CV-430): the selected altitude, Altitude hold, the operating ceiling and the atmosphere use it. The height above the ground is that of the lowest point of the body shape, the capsule of MS-DYN §2.6, with the landing gear not part of it (CV-400, CR-B3-36, which closed TT-820): the hover height `[P-AF-210]` and the minimum operating height `[P-SYS-210]` are heights above the ground (D67, D70), and Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff and Land guide on it. NAV uses the expression of MS-DYN §2.6 with the body dimensions `[P-DYN-020]` and `[P-DYN-030]` of the run's vehicle parameter set, the same expression the dynamics model uses for the ground contact (IN-C1-45). At rest on the extended gear the height above the ground is the gear length `[P-DYN-040]`; at the hover height the extended gear clears the ground by `[P-AF-210]` − `[P-DYN-040]`, which is an autoflight matter (MS-DYN §2.5). NAV gives no height of the gear itself (C6-Q08).

**Horizontal speed is a ground speed (D72 Q1).** The horizontal speed is the magnitude of the horizontal vehicle velocity relative to the ground (CV-480), which is what the panel selector, Heading and the envelope limit `[P-SYS-230]` refer to. The flight software receives no wind (L1-AER-670, L2-AER-DYN-340), so NAV computes no airspeed and none is required of it. The frame L is inertial (CV-140), so the horizontal speed times the heading rate is the lateral acceleration that autoflight bounds by `[P-AF-200]` when the vehicle moves along its heading (D72 Q1).

**No VOR and no flight path angle (D56).** L1-AER-340, -540 and -550 are Deleted and have no NAV children; P-NAV-020, -040 and -050 are withdrawn; CV-560 and CV-610 to CV-670 are deleted.

**Depth and verification.** Each record is a leaf at L2 (`Allocated to: —`): the next layer would be the implementation (D4). NAV computes each quantity in closed form from the exact state; there is no estimator, filter or predictor, and any estimation algorithm introduced with a sensor model (#83) is a design decision, not a requirement (DD-C6-03). The L1 parents are verified by roll-up (D40, D58). Procedure IDs follow the record number (IN-, AN-, TP-NAV-nnn); Phase 5 (V3) writes the procedures. The analyses compare the NAV outputs of each control frame with an independent computation from the recorded frame-start state, within the agreement tolerances `[P-NAV-060]` (lengths), `[P-NAV-070]` (speeds) and `[P-NAV-080]` (angles, differenced by CV-930); they need the operational data, or a per-frame record, at the frame-start steps (IN-C6-50). The **tumbling test run** used by several records is an airborne test start (D73, B3-1) at an altitude well above the ground, with zero fuel, level attitude and a pitch rate, so that the vehicle falls while its pitch angle passes through its whole CV-230 range.

**Coverage of the L1 parents.** Every live L1 record allocated to NAV has at least one NAV child.

| L1 parent | What NAV guarantees, in the parent's terms | NAV children |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L1-AER-430 | NAV takes only the vehicle state from the vehicle model, at the boundary as its ICD defines it, and gives the vehicle model nothing | -200, -210 |
| L1-AER-440 | NAV receives the exact frame-start state and computes the frame's quantities from it, unmodified, before autoflight runs in that frame | -200, -310, -320 |
| L1-AER-450 | NAV gives autoflight each quantity the mode catalogue guides on, the horizontal position of the position-holding modes included | -030, -220, -230 |
| L1-AER-530 | At each control frame NAV computes the altitude, the height above the ground, the horizontal speed, the vertical speed, the heading and the track by CV-400 to CV-550, with the CV-580 validity, from the frame-start state and the run's parameter values | -010, -020, -040, -050, -060, -070, -100, -110, -120, -130, -330 |
| L1-AER-640 | NAV gives the operational data output the navigation quantities of each frame, with their validity and the timestamp of the frame-start step | -250 |
| L1-AER-680 | NAV computes one set of quantities per control frame, ready before autoflight executes in that frame | -300, -310 |
| L1-AER-690 | NAV's outputs depend only on the frame-start state and the run's parameter values, and an invalid track has a fixed value, so NAV adds no variation between repeated runs | -140, -320, -330 |
| L1-AER-880 | NAV gives the panel the navigation quantities with their validity, in SI, the same values autoflight receives, as ICD-FCP defines them | -240, -260, -270 |
| L1-AER-890 | NAV passes the six quantities with their validity to autoflight through ICD-NAV-AF, in SI, with a defined value and validity rule for each, and ICD-NAV-AF defines each item | -100 to -150, -220, -260, -270, -400 |

No record is Derived. The choices inside the records are proposed as DD-C6-01 to DD-C6-04 (`work/C6/issues.md` §4).

### L2-AER-NAV-010

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-010
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the altitude of
  CONVENTIONS CV-430 from the vehicle position of the frame-start state.
Rationale:     The altitude is the guidance quantity of Altitude hold, Go to altitude and the
  level-off of Vertical speed (D56), and it is indicated to the crew (CONOPS §4.3). It is the
  geometric altitude of the centre of gravity above the datum plane, h = −z_D (CV-430); there is
  no pressure altitude. On the ground at rest it is the rest altitude of MS-DYN §2.5, the body
  radius plus [P-DYN-040], not zero: requirements on the clearance of the body above the ground
  use the height above the ground (L2-AER-NAV-020, CV-400).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-530): -010, -020, -040, -050, -060 and -070 compute each of the six listed
  quantities by its CONVENTIONS definition, from the frame-start state (-320) and the run's
  parameter values (-330); -100 to -130 give the CV-580 validity of the two direction quantities.
  Together they are the whole of what L1-AER-530 asks, inside the validity range of CV-580.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-530
Source:        N-080, N-190; CV-410, CV-430
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-010; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110) and of the tumbling test run, the altitude NAV outputs differs by at
  most [P-NAV-060] from minus the down component of the recorded position of the frame-start step.
Parameters:    P-NAV-060, P-DYN-040
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-060 value TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-020

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-020
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the height above the
  ground of CONVENTIONS CV-400 from the altitude and the pitch angle of the frame-start state,
  with the body length [P-DYN-020] and the body diameter [P-DYN-030].
Rationale:     The height above the ground is the guidance quantity of Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff
  and Land (L1-AER-530 rationale, D69, D70). The hover height [P-AF-210] and the minimum operating
  height [P-SYS-210] are heights above the ground, measured below the body (D67, D70): TT-820 was
  closed by defining the height above the ground at the lowest point of the body shape, not at the
  centre of gravity (CV-400, CR-B3-36, D73). The landing gear is not part of the body shape, so
  this is the height of the body, not of the gear. NAV uses h − P-DYN-030/2 −
  ((P-DYN-020 − P-DYN-030)/2)·|sin θ|, the expression of MS-DYN §2.6 that the dynamics model uses
  for the ground contact (IN-C1-45), so NAV and DYN agree on where the body is. It does not depend
  on the roll angle (the capsule is axisymmetric about the body x axis) and is defined at every
  attitude, so it has no validity condition (-150). At level attitude, which every mode holds
  (D57), it is h − P-DYN-030/2. At rest on the extended gear it equals the gear length
  [P-DYN-040] (CV-400, MS-DYN §2.5); at the hover height the extended gear clears the ground by
  [P-AF-210] − [P-DYN-040], an autoflight matter (C6-Q08).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-530): see L2-AER-NAV-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-530
Source:        D69, D70, D73; TT-820; CV-400; MS-DYN §2.5, §2.6; IN-C1-45
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-020; success criteria: (a) at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110) and of the tumbling test run, the height above the ground NAV outputs
  differs by at most [P-NAV-060] from the CV-400 expression evaluated independently from the
  recorded altitude and pitch angle of the frame-start step and the run's [P-DYN-020] and
  [P-DYN-030]; (b) at each control frame in which the vehicle is on the ground, it is within
  [P-NAV-060] of [P-DYN-040].
Parameters:    P-DYN-020, P-DYN-030, P-DYN-040, P-AF-210, P-SYS-210, P-NAV-060
TBD/TBR:       P-DYN-020, P-DYN-030 values TBR (TT-740), P-DYN-040 value TBR (TT-830), Martin,
  2026-11-30 and G2 (C1); P-NAV-060 value TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-030

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-030
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the horizontal
  position of CONVENTIONS CV-410 from the vehicle position of the frame-start state.
Rationale:     Stationary and Hover hold the horizontal position over the ground against the wind
  (D56, D60), Takeoff climbs over its ground position and Land descends over the Hover reference
  position (D69, CONOPS §4.4). The reference position is a horizontal position (glossary), and the
  autoflight share of the position-holding budget [P-AF-160] is measured on it (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS
  §7.4). Autoflight receives no vehicle state (L1-AER-440 gives the state to NAV and FC only), and
  NAV derives every guidance quantity from the true state (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS §5.1), so the
  horizontal position reaches autoflight through NAV. The north and east components are in the
  local reference frame, in m (CV-120, CV-410). L1-AER-530 and -890 do not list the horizontal
  position, although the glossary *Navigation* does; CR-C6-01 proposes adding it (C6-Q01). The NAV
  share of the position budget is zero, because the state is exact (L1-AER-440).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-450): NAV's share of the guidance capability is to give autoflight each
  quantity the mode catalogue guides on: the altitude (Altitude hold, Go to altitude, Vertical
  speed), the vertical speed (Vertical speed, Go to altitude, Takeoff, Land), the horizontal speed
  and the heading (Heading), the track (Heading in a wind, [P-AF-140]), the height above the
  ground (Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff, Land) and the horizontal position (Stationary, Hover,
  Takeoff, Land). This record and -230 compute and pass the horizontal position; -220 passes the
  other six. Together they give autoflight what it needs to guide through each mode of the
  catalogue.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-450
Source:        N-130, N-140; D56, D60, D69; CV-410; glossary *Navigation*, *Reference position*
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-030; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110), the north and east components of the horizontal position NAV
  outputs each differ by at most [P-NAV-060] from the north and east components of the recorded
  position of the frame-start step.
Parameters:    P-AF-160, P-AF-140, P-NAV-060
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-060 value TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1); horizontal position in
  L1-AER-530 and -890 (CR-C6-01, C6-Q01), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-040

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-040
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the horizontal speed
  of CONVENTIONS CV-480 from the north and east components of the vehicle velocity of the
  frame-start state.
Rationale:     The horizontal speed is the guidance quantity that Heading flies at the selected
  horizontal speed (D56), and it is indicated to the crew. It is relative to the ground: [P-SYS-230]
  is a ground speed, and so is the panel's horizontal speed selector (D72 Q1, G2-F03). The flight
  software receives no wind (L1-AER-670, L2-AER-DYN-340), so NAV computes no airspeed. The frame L
  is inertial (CV-140), so the horizontal speed times the heading rate is the lateral acceleration
  autoflight bounds by [P-AF-200] when moving along the heading (D72 Q1). It does not depend on the
  attitude. On the ground it is zero.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-530): see L2-AER-NAV-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-530
Source:        D56, D72; CV-480; N-170
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-040; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110) and of a run of SC-060 with a steady wind across the heading, the
  horizontal speed NAV outputs differs by at most [P-NAV-070] from the square root of the sum of
  the squares of the north and east components of the recorded velocity of the frame-start step.
Parameters:    P-SYS-230, P-AF-200, P-NAV-070
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-070 value TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

```req
  standard vertical speed [P-AF-220], and of Takeoff and Land at [P-AF-240] (D56, D69, D70), and
```

### L2-AER-NAV-060

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-060
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the heading of
  CONVENTIONS CV-530 as the yaw angle that CONVENTIONS CV-260 derives from the attitude
  quaternion of the frame-start state.
Rationale:     The heading is the guidance quantity of Heading and the quantity held in Stationary,
  Go to hover, Hover, Takeoff and Land (CONOPS §4.4), and it is indicated to the crew. It is where
  the nose points, defined by the attitude only (CV-530). It equals the Euler yaw angle for each
  pitch angle other than plus or minus a right angle, and CV-260 gives it a value in that singular
  case too, so the heading has a value at every attitude; whether it is meaningful is its validity
  (-120, -130). This is the derivation the dynamics model uses for the recorded Euler angles
  (L2-AER-DYN-070), so NAV's heading equals the heading of the recorded state (IN-C1-31). The
  value is a direction angle in [0, 2π) rad (CV-520, CV-920).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-530): see L2-AER-NAV-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-530
Source:        D56, D57; CV-250, CV-260, CV-530; IN-C1-31
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-060; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110) and of the tumbling test run, the heading NAV outputs lies in
  [0, 2π) rad; at each such frame at which the magnitude of the recorded pitch angle is at most
  [P-NAV-030], it differs, by the CV-930 difference, by at most [P-NAV-080] from the CV-260 yaw
  angle computed independently from the recorded quaternion of the frame-start step. (Beyond
  [P-NAV-030] the yaw angle is ill-conditioned, so two correct computations may differ by more
  than rounding; there the value is only checked for its range.)
Parameters:    P-NAV-080, P-NAV-030
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-080 value TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1); P-NAV-030 value TBD
  (TT-200; 80° proposed, C6-Q03), Martin, G2 (C6)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-070

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-070
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the horizontal speed of the frame-start state is at least [P-NAV-010], the
  navigation subsystem shall compute, at each control frame, the track of CONVENTIONS CV-540 from
  the north and east components of the vehicle velocity of the frame-start state.
Rationale:     The track is the direction of horizontal motion over the ground, defined by the
  velocity only (CV-540, CV-550). Autoflight uses it in Heading, where the vehicle moves along its
  heading and the track tolerance [P-AF-140] applies, including in a wind (CONOPS SC-060 / wind),
  and it is indicated to the crew. It is undefined at zero horizontal speed and ill-conditioned
  near it, hence the condition, which is the CV-580 threshold; below it the track is invalid and
  its value is fixed (-110, -140). The value is a direction angle in [0, 2π) rad (CV-520).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-530): see L2-AER-NAV-010.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-530
Source:        CV-540, CV-550, CV-580; CONOPS SC-060
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Analysis — AN-NAV-070; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110) and of a run of SC-060 with a steady wind across the heading in which
  the recorded horizontal speed of the frame-start step is at least [P-NAV-010], the track NAV
  outputs lies in [0, 2π) rad and differs, by the CV-930 difference, by at most [P-NAV-080] from
  wrap360(atan2(v_E, v_N)) computed independently from the recorded velocity of that step.
Parameters:    P-NAV-010, P-AF-140, P-NAV-080
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-010 value TBD (TT-200; 1 kt proposed, C6-Q02), Martin, G2 (C6); P-NAV-080 value
  TBR (new TT row, IN-C6-60), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-100

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-100
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the horizontal speed of the frame-start state is at least [P-NAV-010], the
  navigation subsystem shall mark the track valid.
Rationale:     CV-580 bounds the validity of the track by the minimum horizontal speed [P-NAV-010],
  inclusive ("minimum horizontal speed for which track is valid"). Autoflight, the panel and the
  operational data read the validity with the value (L1-AER-890 "with their validity"). The
  threshold has no hysteresis (DD-C6-02): with an exact state the validity is a function of the
  frame-start state alone, and a speed that stays near the threshold is a hold, in which the track
  is not used. The proposed value, one knot (C6-Q02), is the horizontal speed selection resolution
  [P-AF-030], and it is above the landing envelope speed [P-SYS-510], so the track is invalid in a
  hold and at each landing.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-890): the six quantities cross to autoflight through ICD-NAV-AF (-220), each
  with a validity: the track by -100 and -110, the heading by -120 and -130, the other four by
  -150; the value of an invalid track is fixed (-140); each is in SI (-270), with the same value
  and validity as the panel and the operational data receive (-260), and each item is defined in
  ICD-NAV-AF with its unit, frame, range and validity rule (-400). Together they deliver every
  quantity the catalogue guides on with its validity across one defined boundary.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-890, L1-AER-530
Source:        CV-540, CV-580; D55
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-NAV-100; success criteria: in a run that accelerates from Stationary
  through [P-NAV-010] to a selected horizontal speed in Heading and then returns to Stationary,
  and in a run of the reference mission (SC-110), the track is marked valid at each control frame
  whose recorded frame-start horizontal speed is at least [P-NAV-010].
Parameters:    P-NAV-010, P-AF-030, P-SYS-510
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-010 value TBD (TT-200; 1 kt proposed, C6-Q02), Martin, G2 (C6)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-120

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-120
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the magnitude of the pitch angle of the frame-start state is at most
  [P-NAV-030], the navigation subsystem shall mark the heading valid.
Rationale:     At and near a pitch angle of plus or minus a right angle the heading and the roll
  angle are not separately meaningful (CV-260): a small change of attitude moves the heading by a
  large angle. CV-580 bounds the validity of the heading by the maximum magnitude of the pitch
  angle [P-NAV-030], inclusive. Pitch is held within [P-AF-180] of zero in every mode (D57,
  L1-AER-390), far inside the threshold, so the heading is valid in nominal flight. The threshold
  matters in an airborne test start at a large pitch angle and after fuel exhaustion, when the
  vehicle keeps the angular rate it had and can tumble (L1-AER-200). No hysteresis (DD-C6-02).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-890): see L2-AER-NAV-100.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-890, L1-AER-530
Source:        CV-260, CV-580; D57
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-NAV-120; success criteria: in the tumbling test run and in a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110), the heading is marked valid at each control frame whose recorded
  frame-start pitch angle has a magnitude of at most [P-NAV-030].
Parameters:    P-NAV-030, P-AF-180
TBD/TBR:       P-NAV-030 value TBD (TT-200; 80° proposed, C6-Q03), Martin, G2 (C6); P-AF-180 value
  TBR (TT-190), Martin, G2 (C5)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-140

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-140
Level:         L2
Type:          Functional
Statement:     While the track is invalid, the navigation subsystem shall set the track value to
  zero.
Rationale:     At zero horizontal speed the track has no defined value (CV-540), and the arc tangent
  of a zero vector depends on the signs of the zero components in floating point. A fixed value
  makes the output a function of the frame-start state alone, so repeated runs agree (L1-AER-690,
  D59), and keeps NAV without memory: it has no state to initialise (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS F-AER-14).
  Holding the last valid track would carry state across frames and make the value depend on the
  history of the run (DD-C6-01, C6-Q04). Consumers read the validity before the value (IN-C6-10,
  IN-C6-30, IN-C6-40). The heading needs no counterpart, because CV-260 defines it at every
  attitude (-130).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-890): see L2-AER-NAV-100. Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): see L2-AER-NAV-320.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-890, L1-AER-690
Source:        CV-540, CV-580; D59; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS F-AER-14
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-NAV-140; success criteria: in the runs of TP-NAV-100, the track value is
  zero at each control frame at which the track is marked invalid, including each frame on the
  ground and in Stationary and Hover.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Value of an invalid track (C6-Q04), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-200

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-200
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall take, at the start of each control frame, the
  frame-start state from the vehicle model at the vehicle-model boundary, as the vehicle-model
  boundary ICD defines it.
Rationale:     NAV is flight software, and the vehicle state reaches it only across the
  vehicle-model boundary (L1-AER-430). The dynamics model provides the state of the first
  simulation step of each frame, equal to the state it records (L2-AER-DYN-330, IN-C1-30); this
  record is the NAV side of that exchange. The state carries the quaternion attitude, with the
  Euler angles derived by CV-260 (L2-AER-DYN-070), the frames and units of CONVENTIONS, and the
  vehicle acceleration (MS-DYN §3); NAV uses the position, the velocity and the attitude.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-430): NAV's part of the boundary is the vehicle state in (this record) and
  nothing else either way (-210). With the DYN, FU, EF, FC, AF and FCP children, every item
  crossing the boundary is one of the listed kinds.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-440): DYN gives the exact frame-start state (L2-AER-DYN-330); NAV receives it
  (this record), computes the frame's quantities from it without modification (-320) and has
  them ready before autoflight runs in the frame (-310). Together NAV's quantities are those of the
  exact simulated state of the frame-start step, as L1-AER-440 requires.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-430, L1-AER-440
Source:        L0-ASM-010; IN-B1-01, IN-C1-30; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS I-10
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-NAV-200; success criteria: at each control frame of a run of the
  reference mission (SC-110), the vehicle state NAV receives equals, element by element, the
  recorded vehicle state of the frame-start step.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       Vehicle-model boundary ICD home (IN-B1-01, TT-720), Martin, G2 (I1)
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```

### L2-AER-NAV-210

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-210
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall limit its data exchange with the vehicle model to
  receiving the vehicle state.
Rationale:     The boundary list of L1-AER-430 is closed. NAV needs neither the gear state
  (autoflight reads it from the vehicle model directly), nor the mass properties, nor the fuel
  state (the panel takes it from FU), and it commands nothing in the vehicle model. The wind is
  not part of the vehicle state and does not reach the flight software (L2-AER-DYN-340).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-430): see L2-AER-NAV-200.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-430

Status:        Draft
```


### L2-AER-NAV-240

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-240
Level:         L2
Type:          Interface
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall provide the flight control panel, at each control
  frame, with the altitude, the height above the ground, the horizontal speed, the vertical speed,
  the heading and the track of that frame, each with its validity, as defined in ICD-FCP.
Rationale:     L1-AER-880 passes the navigation quantities into the panel, which presents the
  indications: the altitude, vertical speed, horizontal speed, heading and track (CONOPS §4.3,
  glossary *Indication*). NAV gives them in SI; the panel converts them to crew units and rounds
  them (L1-AER-870, CV-760, CV-780). The height above the ground is given too, as one of the
  navigation quantities; whether the panel indicates it is for the panel (IN-C6-30). The
  horizontal position is not an indication and is not given. How the panel presents an invalid
  track or heading is for the panel (IN-C6-30).
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-880): NAV's part of the panel boundary is the navigation quantities into the
```

### L2-AER-NAV-250

```req

  of the frame-start step.
```

```req
Level:         L2
```

### L2-AER-NAV-320

```req
ID:            L2-AER-NAV-320
Level:         L2
Type:          Constraint
Statement:     The navigation subsystem shall compute the navigation quantities of each control
  frame from no data other than the frame-start state of that frame and the parameter values of
  the run.
Rationale:     Perfect state knowledge (L0-ASM-010, L1-AER-440): NAV uses the exact state as it is,
  with no filter, estimate or prediction, and carries nothing from one frame to the next. Each
  quantity is then the closed form of its CONVENTIONS definition (DD-C6-03); an estimator is a
  design matter that a later sensor model would bring (#83, D4). Without memory, NAV has no state
  to initialise (FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS F-AER-14), and its outputs repeat exactly when the state
  repeats.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-690): NAV adds no variation of its own between repeated runs: its outputs
  are a function of the frame-start state and the run's parameter values (this record, with
  -330), and the value of an invalid track is fixed (-140). So, given the same vehicle states, NAV
  gives the same quantities, and it cannot change a verdict.
  Sufficiency (L1-AER-440): see L2-AER-NAV-200.
Parent(s):     L1-AER-440, L1-AER-690
Source:        L0-ASM-010; D59; FUNCTIONAL_ANALYSIS F-AER-14; #83
Allocated to:  —
Criticality:   Not assessed
Verification:  Test — TP-NAV-320; success criteria: (a) two runs of the reference mission (SC-110)
  with the same configuration, initial condition, selection script and TST inputs give identical
  navigation quantities and validity at each control frame; (b) NAV evaluated off-line on the
  recorded frame-start state of each frame of one of these runs, in any order of the frames,
  reproduces the navigation quantities and validity of that frame.
Parameters:    —
TBD/TBR:       —
Owner:         Martin
Status:        Draft
Version:       1
```
